form `export NAME=<literal>` will appear with its literal value. Review before sharing.

./CHANGELOG.md:62:- **Image bumps:** cloudflared `2026.6.1` → `2026.7.3`, Pi-hole `2026.06.0` → `2026.07.2`, Tailscale `v1.98.4` → `v1.98.10`, and the docker CLI used by `gluetun-recover` `26.1-cli` → `29.7-cli`. Verified on the NAS from the branch before merge: all four containers healthy on the new tags, no unhealthy containers anywhere, Pi-hole resolving public and `.lan` names with its `${NAS_IP}:53` bindings published, Tailscale re-registered on the tailnet, and all `.lan` services answering through Traefik. The docker-CLI jump is three majors, so `gluetun-recover` was checked specifically — 0 restarts and it can still drive the docker socket (`docker exec gluetun-recover docker ps` lists containers), which is the only API surface it uses.
./CHANGELOG.md:70:- **Docker no longer leaves host-IP-pinned ports unpublished after a reboot** — the fault that took the entire home network's DNS down on 2026-08-05. At boot the Docker *daemon* restores `restart: always` containers itself (compose is never involved), and bindings pinned to `${NAS_IP}` silently fail to be established while the container starts anyway. Reproduced on three consecutive reboots: **pihole, baserow and therapybot — the only three `${NAS_IP}`-pinned containers — failed every time; all 13 wildcard-bound containers were unaffected.** One failed binding drops the container's whole mapping set, so Pi-hole also lost its `0.0.0.0:8081` UI. Pi-hole reported **healthy** throughout, because its healthcheck digs `127.0.0.1` from *inside* the container — so `docker ps`, health status and `deunhealth` were all blind to it. Fixed by `scripts/boot-compose-up.sh`, run at boot via `scripts/boot-compose-up.service`, which runs `docker compose up -d` across all nine deployed stacks and re-establishes the bindings. DNS is back ~20s after boot (Pi-hole's stack is ordered first); the full sweep takes ~5 minutes.
./CHANGELOG.md:156:- **SETUP.md docker group tip**: kept the existing `sudo` recommendation but added the `usermod -aG docker $USER` one-liner so users who want to skip the `sudo` prefix know how
./CHANGELOG.md:223:- **SETUP.md clone block**: The Ugreen and Synology sections referenced `$NAS_STACK_DIR` in `chown` before `.env` existed, causing `chown: missing operand`. The variable is now set as a shell var at the top of the clone block, so volume2 users change one line and the whole block works. Reported by u/OatStraw on Reddit
./CHANGELOG.md:250:- **Queue-cleanup cron silently failing**: Log path moved from `/var/log/` (which `nasadmin` can't write to on UGOS) to `$NAS_STACK_DIR/logs/`. Also added detection for `importBlocked` and `importPending` items (already-imported packs, executable files, quality mismatches) that were accumulating unhandled
./CHANGELOG.md:377:- **Volume mounts restructured**: qBittorrent, SABnzbd, Sonarr, Radarr now mount `${MEDIA_ROOT}:/data` (single mount). Jellyfin and Bazarr mount specific subdirectories under `/data/`. This is a breaking change for existing users — see UPGRADING.md
./CHANGELOG.md:472:- **Pi-hole fails to start on every reboot**: Pi-hole binds to `${NAS_IP}:53`, but if the IP comes from DHCP, Docker starts before the address is assigned — causing a silent exit 128 that Docker never retries. Removed Pi-hole from unnecessary `vpn-net` network (was causing a secondary race condition). Documented the root cause and fix (static IP on NAS) across `.env.example`, SETUP.md, and TROUBLESHOOTING.md

./tests/mutation/survivors.tsv:10:duc-service/app/manual_scan.cgi	10	if [ -d "$LOCK_DIR" ]; then ==> if [ -e "$LOCK_DIR" ]; then	wontfix	-e also accepts a non-directory, and that is the only state that separates the two: with a regular file at the lock path the real cgi queues a request and answers 'A scan will be started within one minute' while the mutant answers 'A scan is already in progress' and queues nothing; with a directory there both outputs are byte-identical (measured). The stack creates that path only with mkdir (scan.sh:20) and removes it only with rm -rf (its EXIT trap), so the divergent state is not one it can reach - the same class as the -f/-e rows already triaged wontfix in survivors.tsv. In that state the mutant is not the worse of the two either: the real code promises a scan that can never run, because scan.sh's own mkdir fails on the same non-directory and exits 75, so the poller defers that request forever.
./tests/mutation/survivors.tsv:11:duc-service/app/manual_scan.sh	11	[ -d "$REQUEST_DIR" ] || exit 0 ==> [ -e "$REQUEST_DIR" ] || exit 0	wontfix	Same class once more: only a non-directory at the marker separates them. Measured with a regular file at DUC_REQUEST_DIR - real: exit 0, no scan, the stray file left in place; mutant: a full index runs and the file is deleted by the rm -rf in the 0) arm. With a directory at that path both are identical. Nothing in the stack writes a non-directory there (the cgi mkdir -p's it, this file rm -rf's it), and in that state the mutant is doing the ordinary thing the protocol does with a marker rather than introducing a defect.

### ./.devcontainer/Dockerfile
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/base:ubuntu

# Node.js (for Claude Code + Playwright)
RUN curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_22.x | bash - \
    && apt-get install -y nodejs \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Docker CLI + Compose plugin (to manage the project's docker-compose stack)
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
      ca-certificates curl gnupg \
    && install -m 0755 -d /etc/apt/keyrings \
    && curl -fsSL https://download.docker.com/linux/ubuntu/gpg \
       | gpg --dearmor -o /etc/apt/keyrings/docker.gpg \
    && chmod a+r /etc/apt/keyrings/docker.gpg \
    && echo "deb [arch=$(dpkg --print-architecture) signed-by=/etc/apt/keyrings/docker.gpg] \
       https://download.docker.com/linux/ubuntu $(. /etc/os-release && echo $VERSION_CODENAME) stable" \
       > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/docker.list \
    && apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
       docker-ce-cli docker-compose-plugin \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Python3 + PyYAML (for pre-commit YAML validation and fix-radarr-paths.sh)
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
      python3 python3-pip \
    && pip3 install --break-system-packages pyyaml \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# GitHub CLI
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends gh \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# sshpass (for NAS SSH access from scripts)
# dnsutils provides dig (for pre-commit domain checks)
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
      sshpass dnsutils \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Claude Code CLI — install as vscode user to avoid npm permission errors
USER vscode
RUN mkdir -p /home/vscode/.npm-global \
    && npm config set prefix /home/vscode/.npm-global \
    && npm install -g npm@latest \
    && npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code
ENV PATH="/home/vscode/.npm-global/bin:${PATH}"
USER root

# Playwright system deps (for running e2e tests)
RUN npx playwright install-deps chromium 2>/dev/null || true

### ./duc-service/Dockerfile
ARG UBUNTU_VERSION=24.04

####################################
# Temporary image for building Duc #
####################################

FROM ubuntu:${UBUNTU_VERSION} AS build

ARG DUC_VERSION=1.4.5

RUN apt-get update -qq \
 && apt-get install -y -qq --no-install-recommends \
        build-essential \
        ca-certificates \
        checkinstall \
        curl \
        libcairo2-dev \
        libncursesw5-dev \
        libpango1.0-dev \
        libtokyocabinet-dev \
        pkg-config \
 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

ADD https://github.com/zevv/duc/releases/download/${DUC_VERSION}/duc-${DUC_VERSION}.tar.gz .

RUN tar xzf duc-${DUC_VERSION}.tar.gz \
 && cd duc-${DUC_VERSION} \
 && ./configure \
 && make -j"$(nproc)" \
 && checkinstall --install=no --default \
 && cp duc_${DUC_VERSION}-*.deb /duc.deb

###############################
# Final image for running Duc #
###############################

FROM ubuntu:${UBUNTU_VERSION}
SHELL ["/bin/bash", "-o", "pipefail", "-c"]

ARG BUILD_DATE
ARG VCS_REF
LABEL maintainer="Maximilian Köstler <maximilian@koestler.dev>" \
      org.label-schema.build-date=$BUILD_DATE \
      org.label-schema.vcs-url="https://github.com/MaximilianKoestler/duc-service.git" \
      org.label-schema.vcs-ref=$VCS_REF \
      org.label-schema.schema-version="1.0.0-rc1"

COPY --from=build /duc.deb /

RUN dpkg -i /duc.deb \
 && rm /duc.deb \
 && apt-get update -qq \
 && apt-get install -y -qq --no-install-recommends \
        cron \
        fcgiwrap \
        libcairo2 \
        libpango-1.0 \
        libpangocairo-1.0-0 \
        libtokyocabinet9 \
        nginx \
 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/* \
 && rm -rf /var/www/html/* \
 && mkdir -p /database /scan

COPY app/nginx.conf /etc/nginx/nginx.conf
COPY app/ducrc /etc/ducrc

COPY app/duc.cgi /var/www/html/duc.cgi
COPY app/manual_scan.cgi /var/www/html/manual_scan.cgi
COPY app/log.cgi /var/www/html/log.cgi

COPY app/startup.sh /startup.sh
COPY app/scan.sh /scan.sh
COPY app/manual_scan.sh /manual_scan.sh

# Default schedule: everyday at midnight
ENV SCHEDULE 0 0 * * *

EXPOSE 80

STOPSIGNAL SIGTERM

CMD ["/startup.sh"]

### ./tests/e2e/Dockerfile
# Runs the Playwright e2e suite on the NAS itself, where `npm`/`node` are not
# actually installed (the NAS OS only ships a stripped internal Node.js
# runtime for its own admin panel, with no npm) — mirrors this repo's
# existing pattern of containerizing tools the NAS OS lacks (see the
# alpine/git usage in nas-auto-deploy.yml and CLAUDE.md's Deploying section).
#
# docker.io (the CLI, not a full engine) is added so tests/e2e/helpers.ts's
# DOCKER_AVAILABLE gate can shell out to `docker exec`/`docker inspect`
# against the NAS's real daemon via the bind-mounted socket — required for
# the VPN egress/leak/killswitch and zombie-container checks to actually run
# instead of skipping.
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.50.0-noble

RUN apt-get update -qq \
    && apt-get install -y -qq --no-install-recommends docker.io dnsutils \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

WORKDIR /repo

### ./tests/mutation/Dockerfile
# universalmutator, containerised.
#
# pi1 cannot install this on the host: it has no pip and no pipx, python 3.13 is
# PEP 668 externally-managed, and `python3 -m venv` cannot bootstrap pip because
# ensurepip ships no bundled wheels here (the fix, `apt install python3-venv`,
# needs root). Containerising is also this repo's established answer for tools the
# host lacks -- alpine/git for git on the NAS, koalaman/shellcheck for shellcheck,
# the Playwright image for the e2e suite.
#
# Both versions are PINNED on purpose. Generated mutants are only comparable
# between runs if the generator is identical; a floating install silently changes
# what the survivor ledger means. This repo has already paid for a floating
# `npm install` against a pinned base image once (see CLAUDE.md, the Playwright
# browser mismatch).
#
# -slim, not -alpine: universalmutator pulls python-levenshtein, a C extension.
# glibc/manylinux wheels exist for arm64; musl would risk a source build with no
# compiler in the image.
FROM python:3.12-slim@sha256:09f7da3bc104798d0afb40bc08d23ab2da20a76130cec1f2ef170848f5d85217
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir universalmutator==1.14.1
WORKDIR /mnt
ENTRYPOINT ["mutate"]

### ./tests/toolkit/Dockerfile
# The Python and coverage toolkit, containerised.
#
# Same reasoning as tests/mutation/Dockerfile, for the same host: pi1 has no
# pip and no pipx, its python3 is PEP 668 externally-managed, and `apt install`
# needs root it does not have. The NAS is worse -- no npm at all, and a
# containerised alpine/git standing in for git. Containerising a missing tool is
# this repo's standing answer, not a new idea introduced here.
#
# Two tools, one image, because kcov costs one apt line rather than a second
# container:
#
#   python3-pytest  the oracle for scripts/lib/*.py, which were heredocs until
#                   they were lifted out and could not be imported at all.
#   kcov            a ONE-SHOT diagnostic, not a gate. It instruments by file
#                   path, and this repo's signature test idiom (awk-extract a
#                   function body and eval it) produces code with no path, so
#                   kcov reports the most thoroughly tested shell in the repo as
#                   0% covered. See tests/toolkit/README.md for the keep-or-
#                   delete rule that decides whether coverage.sh survives.
#
# Pinned by digest for the same reason the mutator is: a floating install
# silently changes what a measurement means between runs, and this repo has
# already paid for that once (the floating npm install against a pinned
# Playwright base, see CLAUDE.md).
#
# trixie, not bookworm: kcov 43 is published for arm64 there, so this is an
# apt-get install rather than a source build on a Pi.
FROM debian:trixie-slim@sha256:d7e12182ce18b85b93007c1dedf31f2d29e01ccf3182cc4017c709b6259bc132
RUN apt-get update \
    && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
        kcov \
        python3 \
        python3-pytest \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
WORKDIR /mnt

Compose files

### ./docker-compose.arr-stack.yml

#####################################################################
# ⚠️  NEVER use 'docker compose down' - kills Pi-hole DNS!        #
# ⚠️  You WILL lose internet before you can run 'up -d'           #
#                                                                   #
# ✅ Safe restart: docker compose -f docker-compose.arr-stack.yml up -d --force-recreate
# ✅ Or use:       ./scripts/restart-stack.sh                      #
#####################################################################

# Own compose project name so operations on other files (utilities,
# magnetio, traefik) can't accidentally take out this stack via
# --remove-orphans - see docker-compose.tailscale.yml for the original
# precedent for this pattern.
name: arr-core

#########################
# Centralized Volumes   #
#########################
# Explicit `name:` on every volume below pins it to its original physical
# name (arr-stack_<volume>, from before this file had its own project name).
# Without this, an unnamed volume is scoped by project name - renaming the
# project (as this file's `name: arr-core` above does) would silently make
# Compose create a brand-new EMPTY volume instead of reusing the real one,
# which is exactly what happened here in production before this fix (all 12
# volumes below got orphaned into arr-core_* empties on the first recreate
# after adding `name: arr-core` - caught via Jellyfin's StartupWizardCompleted
# flipping back to false).
volumes:
  gluetun-config:
    name: arr-stack_gluetun-config
  qbittorrent-config:
    name: arr-stack_qbittorrent-config
  sabnzbd-config:
    name: arr-stack_sabnzbd-config
  sonarr-config:
    name: arr-stack_sonarr-config
  prowlarr-config:
    name: arr-stack_prowlarr-config
  radarr-config:
    name: arr-stack_radarr-config
  jellyfin-config:
    name: arr-stack_jellyfin-config
  jellyfin-cache:
    name: arr-stack_jellyfin-cache
  seerr-config:
    name: arr-stack_seerr-config
  bazarr-config:
    name: arr-stack_bazarr-config
  decypharr-config:
    name: arr-stack_decypharr-config
  dnscrypt-config:
    name: arr-stack_dnscrypt-config
  pihole-etc-pihole:
    name: arr-stack_pihole-etc-pihole

#########################
# Networks              #
#########################
networks:
  vpn-net:
    driver: bridge
    ipam:
      config:
        - subnet: 10.8.1.0/24
  # Renamed from `arr-stack` -> `arr-core` (label only - same CIDR/ip_range/
  # gateway, no renumbering) as part of the trust-tier network segmentation:
  # this is now the "everything except Magnetio" bridge. Verified live on the
  # NAS before this rename that no other project (e.g. a Baserow/therapy-stack
  # coupling CLAUDE.md previously described) actually attaches to this network
  # by name - no such container, static IP, or directory exists on the NAS.
  arr-core:
    name: arr-core
    driver: bridge
    ipam:
      config:
        - subnet: 172.20.0.0/24
          ip_range: 172.20.0.128/25
          gateway: 172.20.0.1
  # Owned by docker-compose.magnetio.yml (magnetio project) - referenced here
  # only so gluetun can join it. magnetio-addon shares gluetun's network
  # namespace (network_mode: container:gluetun) and needs to reach
  # magnetio-scraper/magnetio-redis, which live on this dedicated network
  # instead of arr-core (highest supply-chain/P2P-risk tier - isolated so
  # Sonarr/Radarr/Jellyfin/etc. can't reach it directly). Deploy order:
  # docker-compose.magnetio.yml (creates magnetio-net) before recreating
  # gluetun here, or gluetun's recreate will fail with "network not found".
  magnetio-net:
    external: true

#########################
# Logging               #
#########################
x-logging: &default-logging
  driver: "json-file"
  options:
    max-size: "10m"
    max-file: "3"

#########################
# Security Defaults     #
#########################
x-security: &default-security
  security_opt:
    - no-new-privileges:true
  cap_drop:
    - ALL

# LinuxServer.io images use s6-overlay which needs CHOWN/SETUID/SETGID/DAC_OVERRIDE
# to switch users during init. Extend default-security with these caps.
x-security-lsio: &lsio-security
  security_opt:
    - no-new-privileges:true
  cap_drop:
    - ALL
  cap_add:
    - CHOWN
    - SETUID
    - SETGID
    - DAC_OVERRIDE

#########################
# Services              #
#########################
services:
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # GLUETUN - VPN gateway. Routes download traffic through your VPN provider.
  # All services below marked "via VPN" share this network for privacy.
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  gluetun:
    image: qmcgaw/gluetun:v3.41
    container_name: gluetun
    <<: *default-security
    depends_on:
      pihole:
        condition: service_healthy  # Wait for Pi-hole DNS before VPN connects
    cap_add:
      - NET_ADMIN
    devices:
      - /dev/net/tun:/dev/net/tun
    volumes:
      - gluetun-config:/gluetun
    environment:
      # VPN Configuration - see .env.example for provider-specific setup
      - VPN_SERVICE_PROVIDER=${VPN_SERVICE_PROVIDER}
      - VPN_TYPE=${VPN_TYPE}
      - WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY=${WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY}
      - WIREGUARD_ADDRESSES=${WIREGUARD_ADDRESSES}
      - SERVER_COUNTRIES=${VPN_COUNTRIES}
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - DNS_ADDRESS=172.20.0.5
      # Both LAN variables apply here, not either/or: LAN_SUBNET is the single
      # CIDR the Traefik macvlan also needs, EXTRA_LAN_SUBNETS is any further LAN
      # subnets. Only list-accepting consumers like this one read the second.
      - FIREWALL_OUTBOUND_SUBNETS=${LAN_SUBNET}${EXTRA_LAN_SUBNETS:+,${EXTRA_LAN_SUBNETS}},172.20.0.0/24,10.8.1.0/24
    ports:
      # Ports for services using network_mode: "service:gluetun"
      # Enables direct local access without Traefik (http://NAS_IP:PORT)
      # NOTE: Sonarr (8989) & Radarr (7878) now run on the arr-core bridge
      # (their own static IPs) and publish their own ports — see those services.
      - "9696:9696"   # Prowlarr
      - "8085:8085"   # qBittorrent
      - "8082:8080"   # SABnzbd
      # FlareSolverr (8191) is deliberately NOT published. It is an
      # unauthenticated headless-browser proxy — anything that can reach it can
      # drive arbitrary outbound requests through this host, so exposing it on
      # the LAN is an SSRF/pivot primitive. Its only consumer is Prowlarr, which
      # shares this container's netns and reaches it at http://localhost:8191/
      # (verified live 2026-08-29 via Prowlarr's /api/v1/indexerproxy). Publishing
      # it bought nothing and cost a hole. Do not re-add without a real consumer.
      - "7000:7000"   # Magnetio addon (docker-compose.magnetio.yml, network_mode: container:gluetun)
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.3
      vpn-net:
      # So magnetio-addon (shares this container's netns) can reach
      # magnetio-scraper/magnetio-redis on their isolated network - see the
      # magnetio-net comment in the networks: block above.
      magnetio-net:
        ipv4_address: 172.22.0.4
    # gluetun's own DNS_SERVER (DNS_ADDRESS=172.20.0.5, DoT to Cloudflare -
    # deliberate VPN leak protection) can't resolve Docker container names,
    # only public hostnames. magnetio-addon shares this netns entirely
    # (network_mode: container:gluetun in docker-compose.magnetio.yml) and
    # needs to reach magnetio-redis/magnetio-scraper by name - confirmed
    # broken live ("getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND magnetio-redis") since nothing had
    # exercised a real stream request before. /etc/hosts entries are checked
    # before DNS, so this fixes resolution without touching DNS_ADDRESS and
    # weakening leak protection for gluetun's other dependents. IPs must
    # match magnetio-redis/magnetio-scraper's static ipv4_address in
    # docker-compose.magnetio.yml.
    extra_hosts:
      - "magnetio-redis:172.22.0.3"
      - "magnetio-scraper:172.22.0.2"
    restart: always
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "/gluetun-entrypoint", "healthcheck"]
      interval: 1m
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 30s  # Give VPN time to establish connection

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # QBITTORRENT - Torrent download client. Downloads files through VPN.
  # Access: qbit.lan | qbit.yourdomain.com | NAS_IP:8085
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  qbittorrent:
    image: lscr.io/linuxserver/qbittorrent:5.1.4
    container_name: qbittorrent
    <<: *lsio-security
    network_mode: "service:gluetun"
    depends_on:
      gluetun:
        condition: service_healthy
        restart: true
    environment:
      - PUID=${PUID}
      - PGID=${PGID}
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - WEBUI_PORT=8085
      - DOCKER_MODS=ghcr.io/vuetorrent/vuetorrent-lsio-mod:latest
    volumes:
      - qbittorrent-config:/config
      - ${MEDIA_ROOT}:/data
    labels:
      - deunhealth.restart.on.unhealthy=true
      - gluetun.dependent=true  # gluetun-recover restarts this if SIGKILLed by a gluetun restart
    restart: always
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "curl -sf http://localhost:8085/ && wget -q --spider --timeout=5 http://www.google.com"]
      interval: 2m

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  jellyfin:
    image: jellyfin/jellyfin:12.0
    container_name: jellyfin
    <<: *default-security
    cap_add:
      - CHOWN           # Create config/cache subdirs on first run
      - DAC_OVERRIDE    # Write to root-owned volume directories
    ports:
      - "8096:8096"     # Web UI
      - "7359:7359/udp" # Client discovery (auto-detect on LAN)
      - "1900:1900/udp" # DLNA
    environment:

    # Hardware transcoding: DH4300 Plus is Rockchip RK3588C, not Intel, so Quick
    # Sync (/dev/dri + render group) does not apply here.
    #
    # RK3588 RKMPP hw transcoding (Jellyfin 10.9+) via the Rockchip BSP kernel's
    # VPU. Confirmed present on this NAS (kernel 6.1.84, all device nodes exist):
    #   /dev/dri/{card0,card1,renderD128,renderD129} /dev/dma_heap/{cma,system}
    #   /dev/mali0 /dev/rga /dev/mpp_service
    # After the stack is up, enable RKMPP in Jellyfin dashboard > Playback and
    # uncheck any codecs the RK3588 doesn't support.
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
      - apparmor:unconfined
      - systempaths=unconfined
    devices:
      - /dev/dri:/dev/dri
      - /dev/dma_heap:/dev/dma_heap
      - /dev/mali0:/dev/mali0
      - /dev/rga:/dev/rga
      - /dev/mpp_service:/dev/mpp_service
    volumes:
      - jellyfin-config:/config
      - jellyfin-cache:/cache
      - ${MEDIA_ROOT}/media:/data/media:ro
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.4
    restart: always
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "curl", "-f", "http://localhost:8096/health"]
      interval: 1m
      timeout: 30s
      retries: 2
      start_period: 30s

  dnscrypt-proxy:

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # PI-HOLE - DNS server. Enables .lan domains and blocks ads network-wide.
  # Access: pihole.lan/admin | NAS_IP:8081/admin
  # For: Home Pro + Anywhere setups
  # Configure upstream DNS: Settings → DNS → Custom = 172.20.0.6#5053
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  pihole:
    image: pihole/pihole:2026.07.2
    container_name: pihole
    depends_on:
      dnscrypt-proxy:
        condition: service_healthy
    ports:
      - "${NAS_IP}:53:53/tcp"  # Bind to NAS IP (not 0.0.0.0) to avoid conflict with host dnsmasq on 127.0.0.1:53
      - "${NAS_IP}:53:53/udp"  # NAS_IP MUST be a static IP — DHCP IPs aren't ready at boot, causing exit 128
      - "8081:80"  # Web UI (local network access)
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.5
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - FTLCONF_webserver_api_password=${PIHOLE_UI_PASS}
      - FTLCONF_dns_listeningMode=all
      - FTLCONF_misc_etc_dnsmasq_d=true
    volumes:
      - pihole-etc-pihole:/etc/pihole
      - ./pihole/dnsmasq.d:/etc/dnsmasq.d
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:false  # Pi-hole FTL needs setcap to apply capabilities
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    cap_add:
      - CHOWN           # File ownership during startup
      - NET_ADMIN       # DNS server binding
      - NET_RAW         # Raw socket access for DHCP/DNS
      - SYS_NICE        # Priority scheduling for FTL
      - SETUID          # Drop privileges after startup
      - SETGID          # Drop privileges after startup
      - SETFCAP         # Apply capabilities to FTL binary
      - DAC_OVERRIDE    # Access config files during startup
    restart: always
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "dig", "@127.0.0.1", "-p", "53", "google.com", "+short"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 2
      start_period: 30s

### ./docker-compose.cloudflared.yml
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# CLOUDFLARE TUNNEL - Secure remote access without port forwarding (Anywhere setup)
#
# What it does:
# - Creates an encrypted tunnel from your NAS to Cloudflare's network
# - Routes jellyfin.yourdomain.com → your Jellyfin through the tunnel
# - No ports exposed on your router = more secure
#
# You DON'T need this if you're only accessing services locally.
# Requires: Cloudflare account (free), domain pointed to Cloudflare.
#
# No web UI - runs silently in background.
#
# OPT-IN, because `ready` is not the same as `configured`. The service sits behind
# the `tunnel` profile, so a plain `up -d` on this file starts nothing until an
# operator supplies cloudflared/config.yml (gitignored -- see config.yml.example)
# and asks for the profile:
#   docker compose -f docker-compose.cloudflared.yml --profile tunnel up -d
# Both scripts/boot-compose-up.sh (every boot) and scripts/restart-stack.sh's
# `all` arm reconcile EVERY compose file in this repo. Unprofiled, that means a
# tunnel with no config to read: cloudflared exits immediately, `restart: always`
# loops it, and the crash-loop consumes the NAS (observed 2026-08-16). Profiled,
# compose skips it and exits 0 with "no service selected" (verified on the NAS).
#
# NOTE: Uses separate project name so compose operations on other files
# don't accidentally stop the tunnel (was causing Error 1033).
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

name: cloudflared

services:
  cloudflared:
    image: cloudflare/cloudflared:2026.9.0
    container_name: cloudflared
    profiles: ["tunnel"]
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    command: tunnel --config /home/nonroot/.cloudflared/config.yml run
    volumes:
      - ./cloudflared:/home/nonroot/.cloudflared:ro
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.12
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "cloudflared", "tunnel", "info", "nas-tunnel"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 30s
    labels:
      - "deunhealth.restart.on.unhealthy=true"
    restart: always
    logging:
      driver: "json-file"
      options:
        max-size: "10m"
        max-file: "3"

networks:
  arr-core:
    external: true

### ./docker-compose.magnetio.yml

#########################
# Magnetio               #
#########################
# Stremio addon for torrent/debrid fallback streams — used alongside the
# stremio-jellyfin addon (docker-compose.arr-stack.yml) so Stremio tries the
# local Jellyfin library first, then falls back to Magnetio's torrent
# scraping + TorBox debrid resolution for anything not already cached.
#
# Built from source (no published image upstream) — source lives in
# ./magnetio/{addon,scraper} on the NAS, synced from
# https://github.com/peterdsp/Magnetio.
#
# Deploy with: docker compose -f docker-compose.magnetio.yml up -d --build
#
# Install in Stremio: visit http://NAS_IP:7000/configure in a browser first —
# v1.1.5 configures providers/subtitles/debrid via a web UI (not a raw
# pipe-delimited manifest-path config as earlier addon versions used), which
# generates the manifest install URL/deep link for you at the end.
#
# magnetio-addon runs on Gluetun's network namespace (network_mode:
# "container:gluetun") so ALL of its traffic — including raw BitTorrent
# peer connections, not just tracker announces — is forced through the
# VPN tunnel. Port 7000 is still published at NAS_IP:7000, just via
# gluetun's ports list in docker-compose.arr-stack.yml now. Magnetio's own
# "P2P Privacy Proxy" (proxy=1|proxyUrl=socks5://...) setting is redundant
# with this and can be left off in the manifest URL going forward — the
# vpn-socks5 container it pointed at is still deployed in case another
# service wants a standalone SOCKS5 proxy later.

# Own compose project name so operations on other files can't accidentally
# take out Magnetio via --remove-orphans. This is also the highest
# supply-chain/network-risk tier in the stack (locally-built, unaudited
# source, raw P2P/DHT traffic) — magnetio-scraper/magnetio-redis also get
# their own dedicated network (magnetio-net, below) rather than sharing
# arr-core with everything else.
name: magnetio

#########################
# Volumes               #
#########################
# Explicit name: pins this to its original physical volume (arr-stack_*,
# from before this file had its own project name) - see docker-compose.arr-
# stack.yml's volumes: block for why this matters (an unnamed volume gets
# silently orphaned into a fresh empty one on any future project rename).
volumes:
  magnetio-redis-data:
    name: arr-stack_magnetio-redis-data

#########################
# Networks              #
#########################
# Dedicated bridge, owned by this file - carves magnetio-scraper/magnetio-
# redis off arr-core entirely (highest supply-chain/P2P-risk tier: locally-
# built, unaudited source, raw DHT/torrent traffic). Sonarr/Radarr/Jellyfin/
# etc. can no longer reach these containers directly. magnetio-addon still
# needs to reach them, but it has no networks: of its own (network_mode:
# container:gluetun) - gluetun joins magnetio-net as an external network
# instead (see docker-compose.arr-stack.yml). Old arr-core IPs .18/.19 are
# freed, not reused.
networks:
  magnetio-net:
    name: magnetio-net
    driver: bridge
    ipam:
      config:
        - subnet: 172.22.0.0/24

#########################
# Logging               #
#########################
x-logging: &default-logging
  driver: "json-file"
  options:
    max-size: "10m"
    max-file: "3"

#########################
# Security Defaults     #
#########################
x-security: &default-security
  security_opt:
    - no-new-privileges:true
  cap_drop:
    - ALL

#########################
# Services              #
#########################
services:
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # MAGNETIO-SCRAPER - Torrent provider scraper backend (21+ built-in sites).
  # No web UI. Queried internally by magnetio-addon.
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  magnetio-scraper:
    build:
      context: ./magnetio/scraper
      dockerfile: Dockerfile
    image: magnetio-scraper:local
    container_name: magnetio-scraper
    <<: *default-security
    environment:
      - PORT=8080
      - REDIS_URI=redis://:${MAGNETIO_REDIS_PASSWORD}@magnetio-redis:6379
      - CACHE_TTL_STREAMS=86400
      - LOG_LEVEL=info
    depends_on:
      magnetio-redis:
        condition: service_healthy
    networks:
      magnetio-net:
        ipv4_address: 172.22.0.2
    restart: unless-stopped
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "wget", "-qO-", "http://localhost:8080/health"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 3

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # MAGNETIO-ADDON - Stremio addon frontend. Resolves torrent streams via the
  # scraper and TorBox debrid. Config is URL-based (see manifest URL above).
  # Access: NAS_IP:7000
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  magnetio-addon:
    build:
      context: ./magnetio/addon
      dockerfile: Dockerfile
    image: magnetio-addon:local
    container_name: magnetio-addon
    <<: *default-security
    # Magnetio's own SOCKS5 proxy setting only wraps tracker-announce
    # traffic, not the actual BitTorrent peer connections that carry the
    # download data (confirmed by reading lib/torrentProxy.js — the socks
    # option only sets opts.tracker.proxy, peer sockets connect directly).
    # Sharing Gluetun's network namespace instead forces ALL traffic —
    # trackers, peer connections, DHT, its own HTTP server — through the
    # VPN tunnel at the OS level, same as qBittorrent/SABnzbd/Prowlarr/
    # FlareSolverr in docker-compose.arr-stack.yml. "container:gluetun"
    # (not "service:gluetun") because gluetun lives in a different compose
    # file/project — Compose only resolves "service:" within its own file,
    # but "container:<name>" works across projects by literal container
    # name. Gluetun must already be running before this starts (no
    # cross-file depends_on possible); port 7000 is published via
    # gluetun's own `ports:` list in docker-compose.arr-stack.yml instead
    # of here, since a container sharing another's netns can't declare its
    # own port mappings.
    network_mode: "container:gluetun"
    depends_on:
      magnetio-redis:
        condition: service_healthy
      magnetio-scraper:
        condition: service_healthy
    environment:
      - PORT=7000
      - REDIS_URI=redis://:${MAGNETIO_REDIS_PASSWORD}@magnetio-redis:6379
      - SCRAPER_URL=http://magnetio-scraper:8080
      - LOG_LEVEL=info
      # Basic-auth for /stats and the swagger-stats middleware. The addon
      # source falls back to admin/magnetio when these are unset
      # (addon/index.js:18-19, addon/serverless.js:153-154) - published
      # defaults, so leaving them unset serves live metrics to anyone who can
      # reach :7000. Verified on the NAS 2026-08-30: unset, and
      # `curl -u admin:magnetio .../stats` returned 200.
      #
      # `:?` and NOT a bare ${METRICS_USER}: an unset variable expands to the
      # EMPTY STRING, and '' is falsy in JS, so `process.env.METRICS_USER ||
      # 'admin'` silently reinstates the default. Confirmed by running that
      # expression inside the real container. The required-variable form makes
      # compose refuse to start instead of quietly serving defaults.
      #
      # Why these two and not every secret in the stack: `:?` is used where an
      # empty value is SILENTLY INSECURE rather than merely broken. These fall
      # back to a credential published in upstream's README, so empty means
      # "wide open and looks fine". MAGNETIO_REDIS_PASSWORD above stays bare
      # because an empty value there fails loudly and visibly instead - redis
      # starts with `--requirepass ''` and every client is refused (verified
      # 2026-08-30: `redis-cli ping` returns NOAUTH). Apply `:?` to a new
      # variable when empty would be quietly unsafe, not as a blanket rule.
      - METRICS_USER=${METRICS_USER:?METRICS_USER must be set - see .env.example}
      - METRICS_PASSWORD=${METRICS_PASSWORD:?METRICS_PASSWORD must be set - see .env.example}
    labels:
      - gluetun.dependent=true  # gluetun-recover restarts this if SIGKILLed/zombied by a gluetun restart
    restart: unless-stopped
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "wget", "-qO-", "http://localhost:7000/health"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 3

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # MAGNETIO-REDIS - Cache backend shared by the scraper and addon.
  # Internal only, no published port.
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  magnetio-redis:
    image: redis:8-alpine
    container_name: magnetio-redis
    <<: *default-security
    # redis:7-alpine's entrypoint uses setpriv to drop from root to the redis
    # user on startup — cap_drop: ALL removes CAP_SETUID/CAP_SETGID needed for
    # that, so add them back (same pattern as lsio-security in
    # docker-compose.arr-stack.yml for images with a similar init step).
    cap_add:
      - SETUID
      - SETGID
    command: redis-server --save 60 1 --loglevel warning --maxmemory 512mb --maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru --requirepass ${MAGNETIO_REDIS_PASSWORD}
    volumes:
      - magnetio-redis-data:/data
    networks:
      magnetio-net:
        ipv4_address: 172.22.0.3
    restart: unless-stopped
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "redis-cli", "-a", "${MAGNETIO_REDIS_PASSWORD}", "ping"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5

### ./docker-compose.tailscale.yml
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# TAILSCALE - Remote access to your full LAN via mesh VPN
#
# What it does:
# - Acts as a subnet router: advertises your LAN (e.g. 192.168.1.0/24) to your
#   Tailscale tailnet so phones/laptops can reach Pi-hole, *.lan domains,
#   Home Assistant, NAS admin UIs, etc. from anywhere.
# - Traverses CGNAT (no port-forward needed) and falls back to a relay over
#   :443 if direct UDP fails — works on hotel WiFi, mobile, anywhere.
#
# When to use this (vs Cloudflare Tunnel):
# - Cloudflared exposes specific HTTP services to the public internet.
# - Tailscale gives YOU (and authorised devices) private access to the whole
#   LAN — including non-HTTP services and admin UIs you'd never expose publicly.
# - The two are complementary, not alternatives. Run both.
#
# Requires: free Tailscale account (https://tailscale.com).
# Setup: see docs/TAILSCALE.md.
#
# Uses a separate compose project name so operations on other files don't
# accidentally stop the tunnel.
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

name: tailscale

services:
  tailscale:
    image: tailscale/tailscale:v1.102.3
    container_name: tailscale
    hostname: ${TS_HOSTNAME:-arr-stack-nas}
    # network_mode: host is required for subnet routing — the daemon needs
    # to see the host's LAN interface to advertise/route its subnet.
    network_mode: host
    # cap_drop: ALL isn't possible here - network_mode: host + subnet/exit-node
    # routing genuinely needs NET_ADMIN/NET_RAW. no-new-privileges is the one
    # hardening flag compatible with that.
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_add:
      - NET_ADMIN
      - NET_RAW
    devices:
      - /dev/net/tun:/dev/net/tun
    volumes:
      - tailscale-state:/var/lib/tailscale
    environment:
      - TS_STATE_DIR=/var/lib/tailscale
      - TS_USERSPACE=false
      # Don't override the NAS's resolver (Pi-hole stays the LAN DNS).
      # Split-DNS for *.lan is configured in the Tailscale admin console.
      - TS_ACCEPT_DNS=false
      # Auth: leave TS_AUTHKEY unset for interactive first-run auth.
      # First start prints a login URL in `docker logs tailscale` — open it,
      # authenticate, then approve the route in the admin console.
      # (Optional) for unattended re-auth: set TS_AUTHKEY=tskey-auth-... in .env
      - TS_AUTHKEY=${TS_AUTHKEY:-}
      # Advertises your LAN subnet (LAN_SUBNET from .env) so tailnet peers can
      # reach the whole network. Add extra subnets with TS_EXTRA_ROUTES if needed.
      # NOTE: --advertise-exit-node is DELIBERATELY ABSENT. Node 1 must never
      # offer itself as an exit node, for two independent reasons:
      #   1. It does not work. Tailscale writes its exit-node rules to the
      #      LEGACY iptables tables (ts-postrouting MASQUERADE on mark
      #      0x40000, ts-forward ACCEPT) while Docker and UGOS use the NFT
      #      backend, where filter carries -P FORWARD DROP and UG_FORWARD
      #      accepts only -i lo plus conntrack RELATED,ESTABLISHED. Nothing
      #      accepts new forwarded flows from tailscale0, so exit traffic is
      #      dropped. Verified twice with a 20s settle: zero egress, DNS
      #      fails, HTTP 000, 0 bytes on bulk download -- while `tailscale
      #      ping` still reports 2ms. Subnet routing is unaffected and keeps
      #      working, which is exactly what makes this so confusing.
      #   2. Fixing it would be WORSE than leaving it broken. A working node-1
      #      exit node egresses via the HOME IP -- precisely the fallback the
      #      leak test (Go/No-Go check I) exists to catch. The exit-node role
      #      is now served by arr-stack-router (native Tailscale + WireGuard
      #      on the router itself, ProtonVPN egress) -- see
      #      docs/EXIT-NODE-PROJECT-LOG.md -- never via the home connection.
      # The user selected `arr-stack-nas` as their exit node by mistake and
      # spent days debugging a phantom "barely connects" problem as a direct
      # result. Leaving the option visible is the footgun; removing the flag
      # removes it from `tailscale exit-node list` entirely.
      # NOTE ALSO: the ACL's autoApprovers.exitNode grants tag:nas-router, so
      # unapproving node 1 in the admin console is NOT durable -- it would be
      # auto-re-approved the moment it advertised again. Not advertising in
      # the first place is the only fix that holds.
      # --advertise-tags=tag:nas-router requires tag:nas-router to already
      # exist in the tailnet's ACL policy's tagOwners (see docs/TAILSCALE.md
      # step 6). Confirmed live 2026-08-17: requesting a tag not yet in
      # tagOwners doesn't just drop the tag with a warning — it fails the
      # entire `tailscale up` authorization, so the node can't log in at all
      # until the tag is removed or the policy grants it. ACL policy with
      # tag:nas-router confirmed live 2026-08-17 before this flag was added
      # back — don't remove tag:nas-router from tagOwners without also
      # removing --advertise-tags here first, same failure mode in reverse.
      # No --reset here (tasks #77/#79, docs/EXIT-NODE-PROJECT-LOG.md §5/§7).
      # It was only ever needed to force-clear a stale AdvertiseRoutes left
      # over from an earlier exit-node experiment; that cleanup landed in
      # a87400c (itself applied via a --reset recreate), so the flag had
      # nothing left to do here except force-wipe prefs back to exactly
      # these compose-file args on every restart — including live-only,
      # not-in-git state (e.g. `tailscale set --relay-server-port=41641` for
      # peer-relay support) that nothing detected the loss of. Guarded by
      # the "does NOT pass --reset" bats test in tests/compose-validation.bats
      # so it can't come back silently.
      - TS_EXTRA_ARGS=--advertise-routes=${LAN_SUBNET}${TS_EXTRA_ROUTES:+,${TS_EXTRA_ROUTES}} --accept-routes --advertise-tags=tag:nas-router
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "tailscale", "status", "--peers=false"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 30s
    restart: always
    logging:
      driver: "json-file"
      options:
        max-size: "10m"
        max-file: "3"

# Explicit `name:` on every volume below pins it to its real physical name
# (tailscale_<volume>, from this file's own `name: tailscale` above). Without
# this, an unnamed volume is scoped by project name - if that project name
# ever changed, Compose would silently create a brand-new EMPTY volume
# instead of reusing the real one (arr-stack.yml hit exactly this in
# production before it added the same pins - see its own volumes-block
# comment). Confirmed live 2026-08-25 that the real names already match this
# pattern (`docker volume ls`), including a stray orphaned `tailscale-state`
# volume (no project prefix) left over from before this file had `name:
# tailscale` at all - that one is unused dead weight, not referenced here.
volumes:
  tailscale-state:
    name: tailscale_tailscale-state

### ./docker-compose.traefik.yml
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# TRAEFIK - Reverse proxy for .lan domains and external access
#
# What it does:
# - Enables .lan domains (e.g., jellyfin.lan) — requires Pi-hole for DNS
# - Enables external access (e.g., jellyfin.yourdomain.com) — requires Cloudflared
#
# SSL: Cloudflare Tunnel handles HTTPS for external access. .lan domains use HTTP.
#
# You DON'T need this for Core setup (IP:port access like 192.168.1.50:8096).
# You DO need this for .lan domains or external access.
#
# Access: traefik.lan | traefik.yourdomain.com
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

# Own compose project name so operations on other files can't accidentally
# take out Traefik via --remove-orphans - it owns the traefik-lan macvlan
# IP and every .lan URL depends on it staying up.
name: traefik-edge

services:
  traefik:
    # Pinned to a patch version, not the floating `v3.7` minor tag. The float
    # was not harmless: the NAS was running 3.7.10 while `v3.7` upstream had
    # moved on to 3.7.12, so the deployed version depended on whenever the
    # image was last pulled rather than on anything in this file. Bump this
    # deliberately; never widen it back to a minor tag.
    image: traefik:v3.7.12
    container_name: traefik
    restart: always
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    # docker-socket-proxy lives in docker-compose.utilities.yml (arr-utilities
    # project), not this file - Compose depends_on can't reference a service
    # in another project, so there's no enforced start-order here. Deploy
    # docker-compose.utilities.yml (or at least docker-socket-proxy) before
    # recreating traefik, or Traefik's docker provider will fail to connect
    # until it retries.
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.2
      # macvlan gives Traefik its own LAN IP where it can use port 80
      # (NAS owns port 80 on its IP, but Traefik owns it on TRAEFIK_LAN_IP)
      traefik-lan:
        ipv4_address: ${TRAEFIK_LAN_IP}
        mac_address: ${TRAEFIK_LAN_MAC}
    # No published ports on the host's IP. The dashboard used to be mapped as
    # 9090:8080, and it served nothing: the static config sets api.insecure:
    # false, so that port accepted a connection and reset it. Measured before
    # removing it -- the API is only reachable through the traefik.lan router,
    # which targets api@internal behind the auth middleware, and HTTP is served
    # on the macvlan address (TRAEFIK_LAN_IP) rather than on NAS_IP. Re-add a
    # mapping only together with api.insecure: true, and say why here.
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
    volumes:
      - ./traefik/traefik.yml:/traefik.yml:ro
      - ./traefik/dynamic:/dynamic:ro
      - ./traefik/certs:/certs:ro
    labels:
      - "traefik.enable=true"
      - "traefik.docker.network=arr-core"

      # Dashboard (external access via Cloudflare Tunnel)
      - "traefik.http.routers.traefik-dashboard.rule=Host(`traefik.${DOMAIN}`)"
      - "traefik.http.routers.traefik-dashboard.entrypoints=web"
      - "traefik.http.routers.traefik-dashboard.service=api@internal"
      - "traefik.http.routers.traefik-dashboard.middlewares=dashboard-auth"

      # Dashboard authentication
      # Generate with: htpasswd -nbB admin 'YOUR_PASSWORD'
      - "traefik.http.middlewares.dashboard-auth.basicauth.users=${TRAEFIK_DASHBOARD_AUTH}"

      # Local admin-UI authentication (Sonarr/Radarr/Prowlarr/qBittorrent/
      # Bazarr/SABnzbd/Pi-hole/Uptime Kuma/duc/Beszel/traefik.lan - see
      # traefik/dynamic/local-services.yml's `-secure` routers). Defined as
      # a docker-provider label (not in the file-provider dynamic/ configs)
      # specifically so it can pick up ${TRAEFIK_DASHBOARD_AUTH} via Compose's
      # own env substitution - the file provider has no env-var expansion,
      # so a file-provider router references this as `admin-auth@docker`.
      # Reuses the dashboard credential rather than adding a second secret;
      # swap in a distinct ADMIN_UI_AUTH var later if these should diverge.
      - "traefik.http.middlewares.admin-auth.basicauth.users=${TRAEFIK_DASHBOARD_AUTH}"
    logging:
      driver: "json-file"
      options:
        max-size: "10m"
        max-file: "3"
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "traefik", "healthcheck", "--ping"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 30s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 60s

networks:
  arr-core:
    external: true
  # macvlan network gives Traefik its own IP on your physical LAN
  # This allows Traefik to own port 80/443 (which NAS can't give up)
  traefik-lan:
    driver: macvlan
    driver_opts:
      parent: ${LAN_INTERFACE}  # Network interface - check with: ip link show
    ipam:
      config:
        # EXACTLY ONE CIDR. Not a list. `docker compose config` does NOT catch a
        # comma-joined value here -- it only interpolates; the grammar is enforced
        # by the macvlan driver at creation time, which is AFTER compose has torn
        # the old network down. A second subnet appended here on 2026-08-30 left
        # Traefik stopped with its network deleted and every .lan name dead.
        # Extra LAN subnets go in EXTRA_LAN_SUBNETS, which only the list-accepting
        # consumers read.
        - subnet: ${LAN_SUBNET}
          gateway: ${LAN_GATEWAY}

### ./docker-compose.utilities.yml

#########################
# Optional NAS Utilities #
#########################
# These services are useful but not required for the media stack.
# Deploy with: docker compose -f docker-compose.utilities.yml up -d

# Own compose project name so operations on other files can't accidentally
# take out monitoring/utilities via --remove-orphans.
name: arr-utilities

#########################
# Volumes               #
#########################
# Explicit `name:` on every volume below pins it to a fixed physical name so
# a future project rename (like the one that added `name: arr-utilities`
# above) can't silently orphan it into a fresh empty volume - see
# docker-compose.arr-stack.yml's volumes: block for the incident that made
# this necessary. beszel-data/configarr-repos are pinned to their real
# pre-rename data (arr-stack_*, recovered after this bug hit them too);
# duc-index/uptime-kuma-data/diun-data never had pre-rename data to recover
# (duc rebuilds its index on every start regardless; diun was crash-looping
# pre-rename; uptime-kuma-data was already empty pre-rename) so they're just
# pinned to their current names to freeze them going forward.
volumes:
  duc-index:
    name: arr-utilities_duc-index
  uptime-kuma-data:
    name: arr-utilities_uptime-kuma-data
  beszel-data:
    name: arr-stack_beszel-data
  diun-data:
    name: arr-utilities_diun-data
  configarr-repos:
    name: arr-stack_configarr-repos

#########################
# Networks              #
#########################
networks:
  arr-core:
    external: true

#########################
# Logging               #
#########################
x-logging: &default-logging
  driver: "json-file"
  options:
    max-size: "10m"
    max-file: "3"

#########################
# Security Defaults     #
#########################
x-security: &default-security
  security_opt:
    - no-new-privileges:true
  cap_drop:
    - ALL

#########################
# Services              #
#########################
services:
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # DOCKER-SOCKET-PROXY - Scoped proxy in front of the Docker socket. The
  # services below need Docker API access (inspect/restart/watch containers),
  # but a `:ro` bind-mount of the socket only blocks writing to the socket
  # FILE — it does nothing to limit which API calls a container makes through
  # it (gluetun-recover already calls `docker restart` through a `:ro` mount
  # today). This proxy is the real access-control layer: only the resource
  # categories below are enabled, everything else (exec, volumes,
  # secrets, auth, swarm, ...) is denied by the image's own defaults.
  #
  # Verified live on the NAS against v0.4.2's haproxy.cfg: each category is a
  # path-prefix allow-rule (e.g. CONTAINERS=1 allows /containers/*), gated
  # first by `deny unless GET || POST=1` (POST=1 unlocks all non-GET methods
  # on whatever categories are enabled, not just literal POST requests).
  # IMAGES=1 was added after diun's real deploy failed with "Cannot inspect
  # image" 403s — diun's core job (checking image digests against registries)
  # needs /images/* reads, which CONTAINERS=1 does not cover.
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  docker-socket-proxy:
    image: tecnativa/docker-socket-proxy:v0.5.0
    container_name: docker-socket-proxy
    <<: *default-security
    restart: always
    volumes:
      - /var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock:ro
    environment:
      - CONTAINERS=1  # inspect/list containers + restart (with POST)
      - POST=1        # required for gluetun-recover's `docker restart`
      - NETWORKS=1    # traefik's docker provider needs network info for routing
      - INFO=1
      - PING=1
      - EVENTS=1      # gluetun-recover watches `docker events`
      - IMAGES=1      # diun needs image inspect to check registry digests
      - EXEC=0
      - VOLUMES=0
      - SECRETS=0
      - AUTH=0
      - SYSTEM=0
      - SWARM=0
      - SERVICES=0
      - TASKS=0
      - NODES=0
      - PLUGINS=0
      - SESSION=0
      - BUILD=0
      - COMMIT=0
      - CONFIGS=0
      - DISTRIBUTION=0
    networks:
      arr-core:
        # .20 is reportedly reserved for Baserow (per CLAUDE.md, unverified
        # in this repo - see docs/ARCHITECTURE.md notes) - using .21 to stay
        # clear of it and everything else in .2-.19, well below the .128+
        # dynamic-allocation range.
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.21
    logging: *default-logging

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # DEUNHEALTH - Auto-recovery for VPN failures. Watches gluetun health and
  # restarts dependent services (qBittorrent, Sonarr, etc.) when VPN recovers.
  # No web UI. Runs silently in background.
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  deunhealth:
    image: qmcgaw/deunhealth:v0.3.0
    container_name: deunhealth
    <<: *default-security
    depends_on:
      - docker-socket-proxy
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - DOCKER_HOST=tcp://docker-socket-proxy:2375
    networks:
      - arr-core
    restart: always
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "/app", "healthcheck"]
      interval: 60s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 10s

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # GLUETUN-RECOVER - Closes a gap deunhealth cannot: services that use
  # network_mode "service:gluetun" lose their network namespace when gluetun
  # restarts to rebuild its VPN tunnel. Some are SIGKILLed and stay Exited
  # (docker's restart policy can't rejoin a vanished netns; compose's
  # depends_on restart:true only fires for compose-driven restarts, not
  # gluetun's own); others keep RUNNING as zombies on the dead netns, reachable
  # on localhost but invisible to the rest of the stack. deunhealth restarts
  # neither (it only touches running-but-*unhealthy* containers, and a zombie's
  # localhost healthcheck still passes). This watcher restarts any container
  # labelled gluetun.dependent=true that is Exited OR was started before
  # gluetun's current start (stale netns), both on startup and each time gluetun
  # becomes healthy again. No web UI. Runs silently in background.
  #
  # KNOWN LIMIT: this only covers a gluetun RESTART. If gluetun is RECREATED
  # (config change + compose up), dependents still reference the old container
  # ID and docker restart fails ("joining network namespace ... No such
  # container") — the watcher logs the failure with the compose command to run.
  # See TROUBLESHOOTING.md "After a Gluetun RECREATE".
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  gluetun-recover:
    image: docker:29.7-cli
    container_name: gluetun-recover
    <<: *default-security
    depends_on:
      - docker-socket-proxy
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - DOCKER_HOST=tcp://docker-socket-proxy:2375
    networks:
      - arr-core
    restart: always
    logging: *default-logging
    entrypoint:
      - /bin/sh
      - -c
      - |
        # A gluetun.dependent container's network namespace is destroyed every
        # time gluetun (re)starts. Two failure modes follow:
        #   1. The container is SIGKILLed and stays Exited — docker's restart
        #      policy can't rejoin a vanished netns. Caught by status=exited.
        #   2. The container keeps RUNNING as a zombie on the dead netns: alive
        #      on 127.0.0.1 but unreachable from the rest of the stack. An
        #      exited-only check misses these — and they are what makes Seerr
        #      lose Sonarr/Radarr (and Prowlarr lose FlareSolverr) after a VPN
        #      reconnect, with every container still showing "Up".
        # So we also restart any dependent whose StartedAt predates gluetun's
        # current StartedAt. Timestamps are compared at whole-second granularity
        # (the first 19 chars of RFC3339 are fixed-width, so lexical order ==
        # chronological order; sub-second fields are RFC3339Nano-trimmed and not
        # safe to compare as strings).
        before() { [ "$$1" != "$$2" ] && [ "$$(printf '%s\n%s\n' "$$1" "$$2" | sort | head -n1)" = "$$1" ]; }
        recover() {
          g=$$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.StartedAt}}' gluetun 2>/dev/null) || return
          g=$$(printf %s "$$g" | cut -c1-19)
          for c in $$(docker ps -a --filter label=gluetun.dependent=true -q); do
            state=$$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Status}}' "$$c")
            started=$$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.StartedAt}}' "$$c" | cut -c1-19)
            if [ "$$state" = exited ] || before "$$started" "$$g"; then
              name=$$(docker inspect -f '{{.Name}}' "$$c")
              echo "gluetun-recover: restarting$$name (state=$$state, stale netns)"
              # docker restart only works after a gluetun RESTART (same container
              # ID). After a gluetun RECREATE the dependent's HostConfig still
              # points at the old ID and restart fails with "joining network
              # namespace ... No such container" — only a compose-level recreate
              # of the dependent can fix that, and this watcher can't run
              # compose. Log loudly instead of failing silently.
              docker restart "$$c" || echo "gluetun-recover: FAILED to restart$$name — if gluetun was RECREATED (not restarted), run: docker compose -f docker-compose.arr-stack.yml up -d --force-recreate $${name#/}"
            fi
          done
        }
        # Recover anything already dead/stale when this watcher (re)starts.
        recover
        # Then react whenever gluetun's VPN tunnel comes back healthy.
        docker events --filter container=gluetun --filter event=health_status \
          --format '{{.Action}}' | while read -r action; do
            case "$$action" in
              *unhealthy*) : ;;        # ignore the unhealthy transition
              *healthy*)   recover ;;  # tunnel is back — revive dead dependents
            esac
          done

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # GLUETUN-ROTATOR - Periodically restarts gluetun so it re-picks a WireGuard
  # exit server from VPN_COUNTRIES, instead of staying pinned to whatever
  # server it first connected to. A plain `docker restart` (same container ID,
  # not a recreate), so gluetun-recover's existing health_status watcher
  # revives every "service:gluetun" dependent afterwards - no extra recovery
  # logic needed here.
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  gluetun-rotator:
    image: docker:29.7-cli
    container_name: gluetun-rotator
    <<: *default-security
    depends_on:
      - docker-socket-proxy
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - DOCKER_HOST=tcp://docker-socket-proxy:2375
      - GLUETUN_ROTATE_INTERVAL_SECONDS=${GLUETUN_ROTATE_INTERVAL_SECONDS:-21600}
    networks:
      - arr-core
    restart: always
    logging: *default-logging
    entrypoint:
      - /bin/sh
      - -c
      - |
        while true; do
          sleep "$$GLUETUN_ROTATE_INTERVAL_SECONDS"
          echo "gluetun-rotator: restarting gluetun (interval=$${GLUETUN_ROTATE_INTERVAL_SECONDS}s) to rotate exit server"
          docker restart gluetun || echo "gluetun-rotator: FAILED to restart gluetun"
        done

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # UPTIME KUMA - Monitoring dashboard. Shows health of all your services.
  # Access: uptime.lan | NAS_IP:3001 (LAN-only, use WireGuard for remote)
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  uptime-kuma:
    image: louislam/uptime-kuma:2
    container_name: uptime-kuma
    <<: *default-security
    cap_add:
      - CHOWN           # Create subdirs in data volume on first run
      - DAC_OVERRIDE    # Write to root-owned volume directories
      - FOWNER          # Set ownership on created files
    depends_on:
      - docker-socket-proxy
    dns:
      - 172.20.0.5  # Pi-hole, so .lan domains resolve (e.g. homeassistant.lan notifications)
    restart: always
    ports:
      - "3001:3001"  # Local network access only
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.13  # For monitoring other containers
    volumes:
      - uptime-kuma-data:/app/data
      # No docker.sock mount - if you add a "Docker Host" monitor in the UI,
      # point it at tcp://docker-socket-proxy:2375 instead of a unix socket.
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "node /app/extra/healthcheck.js"]
      interval: 60s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 30s

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # HOMEPAGE - Unified dashboard with live status widgets for every app.
  # Access: https://homepage.lan only (no host port published — see the note on ports below)
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  homepage:
    image: ghcr.io/gethomepage/homepage:v2.0.0
    container_name: homepage
    <<: *default-security
    restart: always
    # No published port: reached via Traefik at homepage.lan, which routes to
    # this container's bridge IP (traefik/dynamic/local-services.yml ->
    # homepage-lan => http://172.20.0.22:3000) and applies basic auth. The old
    # "3000:3000" publish bypassed that auth for anything on the LAN and served
    # the full service inventory unauthenticated (verified 2026-08-29).
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - HOMEPAGE_ALLOWED_HOSTS=homepage.lan,172.20.0.22:3000
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_SONARR_API_KEY=${SONARR_API_KEY}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_RADARR_API_KEY=${RADARR_API_KEY}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_PROWLARR_API_KEY=${PROWLARR_API_KEY}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_BAZARR_API_KEY=${BAZARR_API_KEY}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_SABNZBD_API_KEY=${SABNZBD_API_KEY}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_QBITTORRENT_USER=${QBITTORRENT_USER}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_QBITTORRENT_PASS=${QBITTORRENT_PASS}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_JELLYFIN_API_KEY=${JELLYFIN_API_KEY}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_SEERR_API_KEY=${SEERR_API_KEY}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_PIHOLE_API_TOKEN=${PIHOLE_API_TOKEN}
      - HOMEPAGE_VAR_UPTIME_KUMA_SLUG=${UPTIME_KUMA_SLUG}
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.22
    volumes:
      - ./homepage/config:/app/config
      # No docker.sock mount - matches Uptime Kuma's minimal-privilege
      # precedent above. Widgets reach apps over HTTP, not the Docker API.
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "wget", "--spider", "-q", "http://localhost:3000/api/healthcheck"]
      interval: 60s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 30s

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # DUC - Disk usage analyzer. Visual treemap UI showing what's using storage.
  # Access: https://duc.lan only (no host port published — see the note on ports below)
  # Note: Re-indexes daily at 4am. Manual: docker restart duc
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  duc:
    build:
      context: ./duc-service
    image: duc-service:local
    container_name: duc
    <<: *default-security
    cap_add:
      - CHOWN           # Nginx chown on startup
      - DAC_OVERRIDE    # Write to root-owned volume directories
      - SETGID          # Nginx master drops workers to www-data (gid)
      - SETUID          # Nginx master drops workers to www-data (uid)
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.14
    volumes:
      - /volume1:/scan/volume1:ro      # NAS data (read-only)
      - duc-index:/database            # Persistent index
    # No published port: reached via Traefik at duc.lan (duc-lan =>
    # http://172.20.0.14:80) with basic auth. The old "8838:80" publish served
    # a filesystem-layout browser unauthenticated to the whole LAN.
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - SCHEDULE=0 4 * * *             # Re-index daily at 4am
    restart: always
    logging: *default-logging


  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # BESZEL - Lightweight server monitoring with historical data and alerts.
  # Access: https://beszel.lan only (no host port published — see the note on ports below)
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  beszel:
    image: henrygd/beszel:0.19
    container_name: beszel
    <<: *default-security
    cap_add:
      - CHOWN           # Create data dirs on first run
      - DAC_OVERRIDE    # Write to root-owned volume directories
    restart: always
    # No published port: reached via Traefik at beszel.lan (beszel-lan =>
    # http://172.20.0.15:8090) with basic auth. The old "8090:8090" publish
    # answered /api/collections/systems/records unauthenticated. Safe to drop:
    # Beszel's hub dials each agent (beszel-agent listens on PORT=45876,
    # network_mode: host), so no agent depends on this port.
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.15
    extra_hosts:
      - "host.docker.internal:host-gateway"
    volumes:
      - beszel-data:/beszel_data
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "/beszel", "health", "--url", "http://localhost:8090"]
      interval: 60s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 30s

  beszel-agent:
    image: henrygd/beszel-agent:0.19
    container_name: beszel-agent
    <<: *default-security
    restart: always
    network_mode: host
    depends_on:
      - docker-socket-proxy
    environment:
      - PORT=45876
      - KEY=${BESZEL_KEY}
      # network_mode: host means no Docker embedded DNS, so this must use
      # docker-socket-proxy's bridge IP (172.20.0.21) rather than its
      # container name - the host can route to bridge-network IPs directly.
      - DOCKER_HOST=tcp://172.20.0.21:2375
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "/agent", "health"]
      interval: 60s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 10s

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # DIUN - Docker Image Update Notifier. Watches running containers and sends
  # push notifications (via ntfy.sh) when newer image versions are available
  # on registries. No web UI. Subscribe to DIUN_NTFY_TOPIC (set in .env) in
  # the ntfy app/website (https://ntfy.sh/<topic>) to receive alerts.
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  diun:
    image: crazymax/diun:4.33.0
    container_name: diun
    <<: *default-security
    cap_add:
      - CHOWN           # Create data dirs on first run
      - DAC_OVERRIDE    # Write to root-owned volume directories
    restart: always
    depends_on:
      - docker-socket-proxy
    volumes:
      - diun-data:/data
    networks:
      arr-core:
        ipv4_address: 172.20.0.16
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
      - DIUN_WATCH_WORKERS=5
      - DIUN_WATCH_SCHEDULE=${DIUN_SCHEDULE:-0 6 * * *}
      - DIUN_PROVIDERS_DOCKER=true
      - DIUN_PROVIDERS_DOCKER_ENDPOINT=tcp://docker-socket-proxy:2375
      - DIUN_PROVIDERS_DOCKER_WATCHBYDEFAULT=true
      # Watch the whole repo, not just the pinned tag: without WATCHREPO diun
      # only reports digest changes of e.g. :v3.3.0 itself, so a new v3.4.1
      # release never triggers a notification (this is how the Seerr v3.4.x
      # releases went unnoticed). INCLUDETAGS limits the scan to plain semver
      # tags so nightlies/betas/rc tags stay silent. $$ = literal $ (compose
      # interpolation escape). SORTTAGS+MAXTAGS cap each scan at the 25 highest
      # semver tags per repo — without the cap diun fetches manifests for EVERY
      # matching tag of all ~24 repos daily (registry rate-limit bait).
      #
      # NOTE: diun's "first check" (which suppresses notifications) is tracked
      # per REPO, not per tag — enabling WATCHREPO against an existing /data
      # DB notifies for every historical tag it hasn't seen (hundreds). When
      # turning this on, move the old DB aside first so the first scan primes
      # silently:  mv /data/diun.db /data/diun.db.pre-watchrepo  (then restart)
      - DIUN_DEFAULTS_WATCHREPO=true
      - DIUN_DEFAULTS_INCLUDETAGS=^v?\d+\.\d+\.\d+$$
      - DIUN_DEFAULTS_SORTTAGS=semver
      - DIUN_DEFAULTS_MAXTAGS=25
      - DIUN_NOTIF_NTFY_ENDPOINT=https://ntfy.sh
      - DIUN_NOTIF_NTFY_TOPIC=${DIUN_NTFY_TOPIC}
    logging: *default-logging

  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # CONFIGARR - Syncs TRaSH Guides quality profiles and custom formats to
  # Sonarr/Radarr. One-shot job: runs once and exits. No web UI.
  # Run manually: docker compose -f docker-compose.utilities.yml run --rm configarr
  # Dry run first: docker compose -f docker-compose.utilities.yml run --rm -e DRY_RUN=true configarr
  # ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  configarr:
    image: ghcr.io/raydak-labs/configarr:1.24.0
    container_name: configarr
    <<: *default-security
    cap_add:
      - CHOWN           # Create repo dirs on first run
      - DAC_OVERRIDE    # Write to root-owned volume directories
    restart: "no"
    networks:
      - arr-core
    volumes:
      - ./configarr/config.yml:/app/config/config.yml:ro
      - configarr-repos:/app/repos
    environment:
      - TZ=${TZ}
