### .github/workflows/ci.yml
name: CI

# The deterministic half of this repo's tooling: everything that can be judged
# from the tree alone, on a runner that has git and docker. Nothing here reaches
# the NAS. The e2e suite, the deploy pipeline, and tests/network-segmentation.bats'
# live-router assertion all belong to nas-auto-deploy.yml -- or skip themselves
# with a stated reason, which the census in the bats job prints.
#
# main is protected (pull requests required, enforced for admins), so this file
# is the only automated check a PR can carry. It is deliberately not a required
# status check yet: that is a policy decision, not a workflow one.
on:
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
  push:
    branches: ['**']
  workflow_dispatch: {}
  schedule:
    # The expensive halves run nightly rather than per push: the regression
    # corpus is 276 entries and runs its oracle twice each, and the discovery
    # sweep generates hundreds of mutants per target. 03:23 UTC.
    - cron: '23 3 * * *'

concurrency:
  # The event name is part of the group on purpose. Keyed on the ref alone, a
  # push to main cancelled the in-flight nightly dispatch that was still running
  # against it -- observed, not theorised, on the first dispatch: the merge that
  # followed the dispatch killed the sweep mid-run. Per-event groups mean a push
  # can only ever cancel another push, and a scheduled run is never cancelled by
  # anything.
  group: ci-${{ github.ref }}-${{ github.event_name }}
  cancel-in-progress: ${{ github.event_name != 'schedule' }}

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  bats:
    name: bats suite
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with:
          # The mutation job diffs against origin/main, and several suites ask
          # git for tracked files -- a shallow clone answers both wrongly.
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Submodules and hooks
        # tests/hooks-installed.bats asserts the symlinks ./setup-hooks.sh
        # creates. A fresh checkout has neither, which is exactly how this step
        # failed on the pipeline's first run -- see CLAUDE.md's live-dispatch log.
        run: |
          set -e
          git submodule update --init --recursive tests/bats-core tests/bats-support tests/bats-assert
          ./setup-hooks.sh

      - name: Run the suite
        # pipefail so a red suite fails the step while tee keeps the output for
        # the census below. This is the whole suite: compose validity, ports and
        # IPs, secret hygiene, env documentation, doc links, YAML, shellcheck,
        # systemd units, every operational script through the stub harness, the
        # containerised pytest run, the mutation corpus freshness check, and the
        # mutation runner's own tests.
        run: |
          set -o pipefail
          ./tests/run-tests.sh 2>&1 | tee "$RUNNER_TEMP/bats.log"

      - name: Census
        if: always()
        # The runner reports executed/skipped/failed and why each skip happened.
        # In a job summary that is the difference between "green" and "green
        # having run nothing" -- the defect class this repo has closed four times.
        run: |
          {
            echo '## bats census'
            echo '```'
            sed -n '/=== census/,$p' "$RUNNER_TEMP/bats.log" || true
            echo '```'
          } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"

  mutation-guards:
    name: mutation guards for this change
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Corpus entries that guard the changed files
        # run-mutations.sh is this repo's proof that its guards can fail, and it
        # is far too slow to run whole on every push. This selects the corpus
        # files that guard the files this change touched, and refuses two ways:
        # an unresolvable base, and a run where every selected mutation was
        # SKIPPED -- which run-mutations.sh itself exits 0 on.
        run: ./tests/mutation/check-changed-guards.sh origin/main

  lint:
    name: workflow and terraform lint
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7

      - name: actionlint (the workflows themselves)
        # Pinned image, matching this repo's pattern for tools a host lacks.
        # Lints both workflow files, including this one.
        run: docker run --rm -v "$PWD:/repo" -w /repo rhysd/actionlint:1.7.7

      - name: terraform fmt -check
        # Formatting only: no init, no providers, no credentials. terraform/ is
        # the devopsarr config-as-code tree; `terraform validate` needs a network
        # init and belongs in the nightly job if it is wanted at all.
        run: docker run --rm -v "$PWD/terraform:/tf" -w /tf hashicorp/terraform:1.9.8 fmt -check -recursive

  heavy:
    name: full corpus and discovery sweep
    # Nightly, or on demand. Not per push: minutes to tens of minutes.
    if: github.event_name == 'schedule' || github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Submodules
        run: git submodule update --init --recursive tests/bats-core tests/bats-support tests/bats-assert

      - name: Full regression corpus (blocking)
        # Every recorded defect must still be caught. This is the gate the
        # per-push job samples from.
        run: ./tests/mutation/run-mutations.sh

      - name: Discovery sweep (non-blocking, time-bounded)
        # universalmutator hunts for code no test guards. Some mutants are
        # equivalent and can never be killed, so this exits 0 whatever it finds
        # and files its survivors in the ledger artifact below.
        #
        # Bounded, because the per-mutant oracle budget bounds each mutant and not
        # the sweep: 13 targets generating hundreds of mutants each, with a
        # containerised oracle, can run for hours against a six-hour job ceiling.
        # A partial sweep is safe by design -- the ledger merge carries untouched
        # targets' verdicts through -- so a timeout costs coverage of the targets
        # that did not run, not the ledger.
        continue-on-error: true
        run: timeout 150m ./tests/mutation/run-generated.sh

      - name: Coverage (a diagnostic, not a gate)
        # kcov reports BLIND rather than 0% for the awk-and-eval idiom; its own
        # README says it is not a gate, so it must not fail the run.
        continue-on-error: true
        run: timeout 20m ./tests/toolkit/coverage.sh

      - name: Publish the survivor ledger
        if: always()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: survivors
          path: tests/mutation/survivors.tsv
          if-no-files-found: warn

### .github/workflows/nas-auto-deploy.yml
name: NAS Auto-Deploy

# Manual only. This replaces the manual "run ./scripts/sync-nas.sh locally,
# eyeball the NAS, merge by hand" workflow described in CLAUDE.md's Deploying
# to the NAS section with the same steps, automated. Deliberately NOT
# triggered on push — that would skip the pre-merge "verify on NAS before
# touching main" gate CLAUDE.md requires for every change, however small.
#
# To run: pick the feature branch in the "Use workflow from" dropdown on the
# Actions tab, or `gh workflow run nas-auto-deploy.yml --ref <branch>`.
#
# Pipeline: bats (fast, no NAS) -> connect to NAS over Tailscale -> back up
# config volumes (GFS retention) -> sync branch to NAS -> recreate changed
# services -> wait for health -> e2e (on the NAS, for full coverage
# including docker-exec-gated VPN/zombie checks) -> merge to main -> sync
# main to NAS. Any failing step stops the pipeline before it — nothing
# merges to main, and main-sync never runs, unless everything before it
# passed on the real, deployed NAS.
#
# Required repo secrets (see docs/BACKUP.md / CLAUDE.md for setup notes):
#   TS_AUTHKEY_CI   Tailscale reusable auth key, tagged e.g. tag:ci
#   NAS_SSH_HOST    NAS's Tailscale MagicDNS name (arr-stack-nas) or IP
#   NAS_SSH_USER    SSH user on the NAS (leoleg)
#   NAS_SSH_KEY     Private key for a dedicated CI deploy keypair (not the
#                   operator's personal key) whose public half is in that
#                   user's authorized_keys on the NAS
#
# Repo setting required: Settings -> Actions -> General -> Workflow
# permissions -> "Read and write permissions" (needed to push to main / merge
# PRs with the default GITHUB_TOKEN).

on:
  workflow_dispatch: {}

permissions:
  contents: write
  pull-requests: write

concurrency:
  group: nas-auto-deploy
  cancel-in-progress: false

env:
  NAS_STACK_DIR: /volume1/docker/arr-stack
  NAS_BACKUP_DIR: /volume1/docker/arr-stack-backups

jobs:
  deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Require an open PR for this branch
        # main is protected: pull requests are required and the protection
        # applies to admins, so the "Merge to main" step at the end of this job
        # can only ever merge a PR. Checking here matters because that step runs
        # AFTER services have been recreated on the NAS -- a run that cannot
        # merge would otherwise leave the host deployed from a commit that main
        # never reaches.
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          BRANCH: ${{ github.ref_name }}
        run: |
          set -e
          if [ "$BRANCH" = "main" ]; then
            echo "Dispatched on main - this run verifies, it does not merge."
            exit 0
          fi
          PR=$(gh pr list --head "$BRANCH" --state open --json number -q '.[0].number')
          if [ -z "$PR" ]; then
            echo "::error::No open PR for '$BRANCH'. main is protected (pull requests required), so this run cannot merge; push the branch, open a PR, then re-dispatch."
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "Will merge PR #$PR once the NAS verification passes."

      - name: Fast static checks (bats)
        run: |
          set -e
          git submodule update --init --recursive tests/bats-core tests/bats-support tests/bats-assert
          # tests/hooks-installed.bats checks for the symlinks ./setup-hooks.sh
          # creates - a one-time local-dev-machine step nobody runs on a fresh
          # CI checkout. Run it here so that check verifies the script itself
          # still works, instead of failing on every CI run for an unrelated
          # reason.
          ./setup-hooks.sh
          npm run test:bats

      - name: Mutation-check the guards this change touches
        # run-mutations.sh is this repo's proof that its guards can fail, and nothing
        # ran it until now: a corpus entry whose pattern stopped matching reports ERROR,
        # but only to whoever runs it. Scoped to the corpus files that guard the files
        # this change touched -- the full corpus is 271 entries, each running its oracle
        # twice. fetch-depth: 0 above is what makes origin/main resolvable here.
        run: |
          set -e
          ./tests/mutation/check-changed-guards.sh origin/main

      - name: Connect to Tailscale
        uses: tailscale/github-action@v3
        with:
          authkey: ${{ secrets.TS_AUTHKEY_CI }}
          # Unique per run: a fixed hostname collides with the previous run's
          # still-registered (offline, non-ephemeral) device and gets silently
          # suffixed (-1, -2, ...) by Tailscale, cluttering the tailnet with
          # stale entries — seen as both gh-actions-arr-stack and
          # gh-actions-arr-stack-1 in run 31945945854's `tailscale status`.
          hostname: gh-actions-arr-stack-${{ github.run_id }}

      - name: Configure SSH to NAS
        env:
          NAS_SSH_KEY: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_KEY }}
          NAS_SSH_HOST: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_HOST }}
        run: |
          set -e
          mkdir -p ~/.ssh
          chmod 700 ~/.ssh
          printf '%s\n' "$NAS_SSH_KEY" > ~/.ssh/id_ed25519
          chmod 600 ~/.ssh/id_ed25519
          # MagicDNS can take a moment to become resolvable right after joining.
          # Loop must hard-fail if it never gets a key: a bare `&& break` with no
          # post-loop check exits 0 even after 10 failed attempts, which masked a
          # real connectivity failure as a green step on 2026-08-16's run
          # 31945201657 (the actual ssh call two steps later then burned the full
          # ~2m OS TCP timeout before failing for real).
          #
          # `tailscale ping` before each keyscan attempt: run 31945945854 showed
          # `tailscale status`/`ping` succeeding with a direct connection right
          # after 10 straight ssh-keyscan failures, meaning the WireGuard
          # peer-to-peer path to the NAS hadn't been established yet when the
          # raw TCP attempts ran. Plain TCP tools (ssh-keyscan, ssh) don't
          # trigger/wait for that handshake; `tailscale ping` actively forces
          # and confirms it, so running it first warms the route before the
          # keyscan attempt that follows.
          got_key=false
          for i in $(seq 1 10); do
            echo "Attempt $i/10: warming Tailscale route to $NAS_SSH_HOST"
            tailscale ping -c 1 "$NAS_SSH_HOST" || true
            if ssh-keyscan -H "$NAS_SSH_HOST" >> ~/.ssh/known_hosts 2>/tmp/keyscan-err; then
              if [ -s ~/.ssh/known_hosts ]; then
                got_key=true
                break
              fi
            fi
            cat /tmp/keyscan-err
            sleep 3
          done
          echo "--- tailscale status ---"
          tailscale status || true
          echo "--- tailscale ping (4x) ---"
          tailscale ping -c 4 "$NAS_SSH_HOST" || true
          if [ "$got_key" != true ]; then
            echo "::error::ssh-keyscan never reached $NAS_SSH_HOST after 10 attempts — see tailscale status/ping output above."
            exit 1
          fi

      - name: Determine changed compose files
        id: changes
        run: |
          set -e
          git fetch origin main --quiet
          CHANGED=$(git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD -- 'docker-compose.*.yml' | tr '\n' ' ')
          echo "files=${CHANGED}" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          echo "Changed compose files: ${CHANGED:-none}"

      - name: Back up config volumes (GFS retention)
        env:
          NAS_HOST: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_HOST }}
          NAS_USER: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_USER }}
        run: |
          set -e
          ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 "${NAS_USER}@${NAS_HOST}" "
            set -e
            mkdir -p '${NAS_BACKUP_DIR}'
            cd '${NAS_STACK_DIR}'
            # arr-backup.sh stamps YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS itself as of 2026-08-30, so the
            # rename this step used to do is gone. No --rotate-days here: this
            # directory's retention belongs to backup-prune.sh alone.
            ./scripts/arr-backup.sh --tar '${NAS_BACKUP_DIR}'
            ./scripts/backup-prune.sh '${NAS_BACKUP_DIR}'
          "

      - name: Sync branch to NAS
        env:
          NAS_HOST: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_HOST }}
          NAS_USER: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_USER }}
          BRANCH: ${{ github.ref_name }}
        run: |
          set -e
          ARRGIT="docker run --rm -v '${NAS_STACK_DIR}:/repo' -w /repo alpine/git -c safe.directory=/repo"
          ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 "${NAS_USER}@${NAS_HOST}" "
            ${ARRGIT} fetch origin '${BRANCH}' &&
            ${ARRGIT} checkout '${BRANCH}' &&
            ${ARRGIT} pull --ff-only origin '${BRANCH}'
          "

      # The bats step above runs on ubuntu-latest, where there is no .env, so
      # the LAN_SUBNET/EXTRA_LAN_SUBNETS grammar guard silently falls back to
      # the always-clean committed .env.example and cannot fail. The recreate
      # below runs against the NAS's REAL .env. That is the exact gap that let
      # LAN_SUBNET=192.168.110.0/24,192.168.120.0/24 sit dormant until a
      # routine image pin recreated Traefik, which deleted the traefik-lan
      # macvlan and then could not rebuild it -- every .lan hostname went down.
      #
      # `docker compose config` does NOT catch this: it only interpolates, and
      # the CIDR grammar is enforced by the network driver at creation time,
      # i.e. after the old network is already torn down. So the check has to be
      # this one, run here: after the sync (so it tests the branch's own guard)
      # and before anything is recreated.
      #
      # Runs the whole file rather than a --filter regex on purpose: a filter
      # that stops matching after a test is renamed would run zero tests and
      # still exit 0, which is the same could-not-fail defect this guard exists
      # to prevent. A hardcoded count is deliberately not asserted here either --
      # that is how the old "14 tests" claim in CLAUDE.md went stale.
      #
      # run-tests.sh bootstraps bats from the vendored submodule -- the NAS has
      # no npm, and no host git either, so if tests/bats-core is ever missing
      # from the deploy path this step fails loudly rather than skipping.
      - name: Validate the NAS .env before recreating anything
        env:
          NAS_HOST: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_HOST }}
          NAS_USER: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_USER }}
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          # ConnectTimeout bounds the handshake; `timeout` bounds the remote
          # command. Without the second, a stalled suite on the NAS holds the
          # session open until the job's 6-hour ceiling instead of failing in
          # minutes -- the slow-hang shape this repo already fixed once for
          # plain ssh connects.
          ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 "${NAS_USER}@${NAS_HOST}" \
            "cd '${NAS_STACK_DIR}' && timeout 300 ./tests/run-tests.sh tests/env-vars.bats"

      - name: Recreate affected services
        if: steps.changes.outputs.files != ''
        env:
          NAS_HOST: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_HOST }}
          NAS_USER: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_USER }}
          CHANGED_FILES: ${{ steps.changes.outputs.files }}
        run: |
          set -e
          for f in $CHANGED_FILES; do
            echo "Recreating services from $f"
            # Deliberately no --remove-orphans: this project shares one compose
            # project name across multiple files, and --remove-orphans deletes
            # every container defined in the *other* files (see CLAUDE.md).
            ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 "${NAS_USER}@${NAS_HOST}" "cd '${NAS_STACK_DIR}' && docker compose -f '$f' up -d --build"
          done

      - name: Wait for container health
        env:
          NAS_HOST: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_HOST }}
          NAS_USER: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_USER }}
        run: |
          set -e
          ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 "${NAS_USER}@${NAS_HOST}" '
            for i in $(seq 1 30); do
              STARTING=$(docker ps --filter "health=starting" --format "{{.Names}}")
              if [ -z "$STARTING" ]; then
                UNHEALTHY=$(docker ps --filter "health=unhealthy" --format "{{.Names}}")
                if [ -n "$UNHEALTHY" ]; then
                  echo "Unhealthy containers: $UNHEALTHY"
                  exit 1
                fi
                echo "All containers healthy."
                exit 0
              fi
              sleep 5
            done
            echo "Timed out waiting for containers to leave starting state: $STARTING"
            exit 1
          '

      - name: Run e2e verification on the NAS
        env:
          NAS_HOST: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_HOST }}
          NAS_USER: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_USER }}
        run: |
          set -e
          # Runs ON the NAS (not from this runner) so docker-exec-gated tests
          # (VPN egress/leak/killswitch, zombie-container checks) actually run
          # instead of skipping — see tests/e2e/helpers.ts DOCKER_AVAILABLE.
          #
          # The NAS OS has no npm (only a stripped internal Node.js runtime
          # for its own admin panel — confirmed 2026-08-16, node v18.20.4 at
          # /usr/bin/node with no npm anywhere on the system), so the suite
          # runs inside tests/e2e/Dockerfile's Playwright+docker-cli image
          # instead, matching this repo's existing containerized-tool pattern
          # (alpine/git above). --network host so localhost:<port> URLs in
          # tests/e2e/helpers.ts resolve to the NAS's own published ports.
          #
          # `npx playwright install chromium` runs every time (not baked into
          # the Dockerfile): package-lock.json is gitignored, so `npm install`
          # floats @playwright/test to whatever's newest on each run, which
          # can drift past the browser build baked into the pinned
          # mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.50.0-noble base image — seen live
          # in run 31959525960 (npm resolved 1.62.1, image only had 1.50.0's
          # browser, all UI-screenshot tests failed with "Executable doesn't
          # exist"). Installing at runtime always matches whatever npm
          # actually resolved.
          #
          # No --dns / --add-host needed: tests/e2e/networking.spec.ts's
          # Traefik-routing test used to rely on real .lan DNS resolution
          # when following an app's login-redirect Location header (which
          # can't reuse the initial request's "connect by IP, spoof Host"
          # trick) — that chased through ENOTFOUND (run 31967698968, fixed
          # with --dns), then a Pi-hole AAAA quirk landing on the NAS's own
          # UGOS admin panel instead of failing (run 31968540414 and
          # 31968968267 — NODE_OPTIONS=--dns-result-order=ipv4first and
          # --add-host both fixed a plain `node -e dns.lookup()` call but not
          # Playwright's own APIRequestContext, which resolves via
          # dns.resolve4/6 and bypasses both). Fixed at the actual source
          # instead: the test now disables auto-redirect-follow and re-issues
          # the follow-up request by path against TRAEFIK_LAN_IP with the
          # same spoofed Host header, so it never needs real DNS at all — the
          # only remaining .lan DNS test (`dig @<NAS_IP> jellyfin.lan`)
          # already queries Pi-hole directly by IP, not via this container's
          # own resolver.
          ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 "${NAS_USER}@${NAS_HOST}" "
            cd '${NAS_STACK_DIR}' &&
            docker build -t arr-stack-e2e -f tests/e2e/Dockerfile . &&
            docker run --rm --network host \
              -v '${NAS_STACK_DIR}:/repo' \
              -v /var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock \
              -w /repo \
              arr-stack-e2e \
              bash -c 'npm install && npx playwright install chromium && npx playwright test --config tests/e2e/playwright.config.ts'
          "

      - name: Merge to main
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          BRANCH: ${{ github.ref_name }}
        run: |
          set -e
          if [ "$BRANCH" = "main" ]; then
            echo "Dispatched on main - nothing to merge."
            exit 0
          fi
          PR_NUMBER=$(gh pr list --head "$BRANCH" --state open --json number -q '.[0].number')
          if [ -z "$PR_NUMBER" ]; then
            echo "::error::No open PR for '$BRANCH'. main is protected, so the direct-push fallback this step used to have is gone; the preflight should have stopped the run before the NAS was touched."
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "Squash-merging PR #$PR_NUMBER"
          gh pr merge "$PR_NUMBER" --squash --delete-branch=false

      - name: Sync main to NAS
        env:
          NAS_HOST: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_HOST }}
          NAS_USER: ${{ secrets.NAS_SSH_USER }}
        run: |
          set -e
          ARRGIT="docker run --rm -v '${NAS_STACK_DIR}:/repo' -w /repo alpine/git -c safe.directory=/repo"
          ssh -o ConnectTimeout=15 "${NAS_USER}@${NAS_HOST}" "
            ${ARRGIT} fetch origin main &&
            ${ARRGIT} checkout main &&
            ${ARRGIT} merge --ff-only origin/main
          "

Git hooks

### .git/hooks/post-merge
#!/bin/bash
# post-merge hook: after a local merge lands on `main`, get that commit onto
# the NAS. See scripts/sync-nas.sh for why the NAS pulls rather than receives.
#
# Never blocks or fails the merge -- it always exits 0 -- but it is LOUD about
# anything it could not do. A hook that reports nothing is indistinguishable
# from a hook that is not installed, which is the bug this file used to have.
set -uo pipefail

# --- Resolve the repo root ------------------------------------------------
#
# Git invokes hooks with the working directory at the top of the work tree, so
# a plain `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` is the primary route.
#
# The obvious-looking alternative -- deriving it from ${BASH_SOURCE[0]} -- is
# what silently broke this hook for its entire life, measured 2026-08-31: git
# invokes the hook by its path inside the git dir (.git/hooks/post-merge), bash
# does NOT resolve that symlink, so `dirname` yields `.git/hooks`, and
# `git -C .git/hooks rev-parse --show-toplevel` fatals with "this operation
# must be run in a work tree". That error was swallowed by `|| true`, the empty
# result hit an `exit 0` guard meant for "not a git repo", and every merge
# reported success while syncing nothing. The lesson is in the ordering below:
# ask git where the work tree is, and only fall back to path arithmetic --
# resolved THROUGH the symlink, so it lands in scripts/ and not .git/hooks/.
REPO_ROOT="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || true)"
if [[ -z "$REPO_ROOT" ]]; then
    resolved="$(readlink -f "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" 2>/dev/null || true)"
    if [[ -n "$resolved" ]]; then
        REPO_ROOT="$(git -C "$(dirname "$resolved")" rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || true)"
    fi
fi
if [[ -z "$REPO_ROOT" ]]; then
    # Loud, not silent. The previous version exited 0 here without a word,
    # which is exactly why nobody noticed the hook had never run.
    echo "post-merge: cannot determine the repo root - NAS NOT synced" >&2
    exit 0
fi

BRANCH="$(git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD 2>/dev/null || echo "")"
[[ "$BRANCH" != "main" ]] && exit 0

LOCAL="$(git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse HEAD 2>/dev/null || echo "")"
REMOTE="$(git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse "origin/$BRANCH" 2>/dev/null || echo "")"

if [[ "$LOCAL" != "$REMOTE" ]]; then
    # The NAS pulls from origin, so it cannot reach a commit origin does not
    # have. This hook runs DURING `git merge`, before any push -- so on a local
    # merge this branch is always taken. Pushing here is what closes the loop;
    # the alternative is printing a reminder and leaving the NAS on the old
    # commit until someone acts on it, which is the divergence that cost 38
    # hours once already.
    #
    # Guards, in order of what they prevent:
    #   - a dirty tree means the merge is not actually finished; publishing it
    #     would push something the working copy disagrees with;
    #   - a non-fast-forward means origin moved (a `gh pr merge`, say) and this
    #     push would either be rejected or need force. Never force from a hook.
    if ! git -C "$REPO_ROOT" diff --quiet || ! git -C "$REPO_ROOT" diff --cached --quiet; then
        echo "post-merge: working tree is dirty - NOT pushing, NAS NOT synced" >&2
        echo "post-merge: commit or stash, then: git push origin $BRANCH && ./scripts/sync-nas.sh" >&2
        exit 0
    fi
    if [[ -n "$REMOTE" ]] && ! git -C "$REPO_ROOT" merge-base --is-ancestor "$REMOTE" "$LOCAL"; then
        echo "post-merge: $BRANCH has diverged from origin/$BRANCH - NOT pushing, NAS NOT synced" >&2
        echo "post-merge: reconcile with origin first, then: ./scripts/sync-nas.sh" >&2
        exit 0
    fi
    echo "post-merge: pushing $BRANCH to origin so the NAS can pull it ..."
    # Output goes to a file rather than a variable, and the `if ! git ...
    # push` shape is preserved deliberately: tests/mutation/corpus/nas-sync.sh's
    # hook-skips-the-push entry anchors its sed on exactly that line, and
    # tests/mutation-corpus.bats is what caught this refactor breaking it. A
    # rewrite that reads better but moves the anchor silently retires the proof
    # that the hook's push can fail.
    push_log="$(mktemp)"
    if ! git -C "$REPO_ROOT" push origin "$BRANCH" >"$push_log" 2>&1; then
        cat "$push_log" >&2
        echo "post-merge: push FAILED - NAS NOT synced" >&2
        # main is protected (pull requests required, enforced for admins), so a
        # local merge can no longer reach origin at all. The remote says
        # "protected branch" / "must be made through a pull request"; anything
        # else is an ordinary push failure and gets the ordinary advice.
        if grep -qiE 'protected branch|GH006|pull request' "$push_log"; then
            rm -f "$push_log"
            echo "post-merge: $BRANCH is protected - a local merge cannot be pushed any more." >&2
            echo "post-merge: do not force it. Recover with: git reset --hard origin/$BRANCH" >&2
            echo "post-merge: then land the work through a PR:" >&2
            echo "post-merge:   git push origin <your-branch> && gh pr create --fill && gh pr merge <n>" >&2
            echo "post-merge:   git fetch origin $BRANCH && git merge --ff-only origin/$BRANCH && ./scripts/sync-nas.sh" >&2
            exit 0
        fi
        rm -f "$push_log"
        echo "post-merge: fix the push, then: ./scripts/sync-nas.sh" >&2
        exit 0
    fi
    rm -f "$push_log"
fi

if ! "$REPO_ROOT/scripts/sync-nas.sh"; then
    echo "post-merge: NAS sync FAILED (the merge itself is fine)" >&2
    echo "post-merge: re-run manually: ./scripts/sync-nas.sh" >&2
fi
exit 0

### .git/hooks/pre-commit
#!/bin/bash
# Pre-commit hook for ultimate-arr-stack
# Validates commits for security and consistency
#
# Installation: ./setup-hooks.sh
# Manual test:  ./scripts/pre-commit

set -e

# Get script directory (resolving symlinks) and repo root
SCRIPT_PATH="${BASH_SOURCE[0]}"
while [[ -L "$SCRIPT_PATH" ]]; do
    SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$SCRIPT_PATH")" && pwd)"
    SCRIPT_PATH="$(readlink "$SCRIPT_PATH")"
    [[ "$SCRIPT_PATH" != /* ]] && SCRIPT_PATH="$SCRIPT_DIR/$SCRIPT_PATH"
done
SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$SCRIPT_PATH")" && pwd)"
REPO_ROOT="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || echo "$SCRIPT_DIR/..")"

# Color output (disabled if not interactive)
if [[ -t 1 ]]; then
    RED='\033[0;31m'
    GREEN='\033[0;32m'
    YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
    BLUE='\033[0;34m'
    NC='\033[0m'
else
    RED='' GREEN='' YELLOW='' BLUE='' NC=''
fi

# Source common library first (caches NAS config for all checks)
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/common.sh"

# Source helper functions
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-secrets.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-env-vars.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-yaml-syntax.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-conflicts.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-hardcoded-domain.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-env-backup.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-uptime-monitors.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-dns-duplicates.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-domains.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-image-versions.sh"
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-doc-links.sh"

# Track results
ERRORS=0
WARNINGS=0

echo ""
echo -e "${BLUE}Pre-commit validation${NC}"
echo "=========================================="
echo ""

# Check if there are staged changes
STAGED_FILES=$(git diff --cached --name-only --diff-filter=ACM 2>/dev/null || true)
if [[ -z "$STAGED_FILES" ]]; then
    echo "No files staged for commit."
    echo ""
    exit 0
fi

# ==========================================
# CHECK 1: Secret Detection (BLOCKS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}1. Checking for secrets...${NC}"
if check_secrets; then
    echo -e "    ${GREEN}OK${NC}: No secrets detected"
else
    ERRORS=$((ERRORS + 1))
fi
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 2: Environment Variable Coverage (BLOCKS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}2. Checking environment variable documentation...${NC}"
if check_env_vars; then
    echo -e "    ${GREEN}OK${NC}: All compose variables documented"
else
    # Note: check_env_vars outputs its own errors
    ERRORS=$((ERRORS + 1))
fi
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 3: YAML Syntax (BLOCKS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}3. Checking YAML syntax...${NC}"
if check_yaml_syntax; then
    echo -e "    ${GREEN}OK${NC}: YAML syntax valid"
else
    ERRORS=$((ERRORS + 1))
fi
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 4: Port/IP Conflicts (BLOCKS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}4. Checking for port/IP conflicts...${NC}"
if check_conflicts; then
    echo -e "    ${GREEN}OK${NC}: No conflicts detected"
else
    ERRORS=$((ERRORS + 1))
fi
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 5: Hardcoded Domain (WARNS on domain, BLOCKS on NAS hostname)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}5. Checking for hardcoded domain...${NC}"
# Mixed contract, so the return value has to be read. A hardcoded *domain* is a
# warning and returns 0; the NAS *hostname* is private and returns 1, which
# check-hardcoded-domain.sh:58 documents as blocking. This used to be a bare
# call, which discarded that distinction -- and under `set -e` a bare call that
# returns 1 kills the hook outright, skipping checks 6-11 and the summary.
if check_hardcoded_domain; then
    :
else
    ERRORS=$((ERRORS + 1))
fi
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 6: NAS .env Backup Sync (WARNS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}6. Checking .env.nas.backup sync...${NC}"
check_env_backup
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 7: Uptime Kuma Monitors (WARNS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}7. Checking Uptime Kuma monitors...${NC}"
check_uptime_monitors
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 8: DNS Duplicates (WARNS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}8. Checking for duplicate .lan domains...${NC}"
check_dns_duplicates
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 9: Domain Accessibility (WARNS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}9. Checking domain accessibility...${NC}"
check_domains
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 10: Image Version Staleness (WARNS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}10. Checking for image updates...${NC}"
# Timeout after 30s to prevent slow registries from blocking commits
_timeout_cmd=""
command -v timeout &>/dev/null && _timeout_cmd="timeout"
command -v gtimeout &>/dev/null && _timeout_cmd="gtimeout"
if [[ -n "$_timeout_cmd" ]]; then
    $_timeout_cmd 30 bash -c "source '$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/common.sh'; source '$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/check-image-versions.sh'; check_image_versions" 2>/dev/null || {
        [[ $? -eq 124 ]] && echo "    SKIP: Registry checks timed out (30s)"
    }
else
    check_image_versions
fi
echo ""

# ==========================================
# CHECK 11: Documentation Links (BLOCKS)
# ==========================================
echo -e "${BLUE}11. Checking documentation links...${NC}"
if check_doc_links; then
    echo -e "    ${GREEN}OK${NC}: All internal links valid"
else
    ERRORS=$((ERRORS + 1))
fi
echo ""

# ==========================================
# SUMMARY
# ==========================================
echo "=========================================="
if [[ $ERRORS -gt 0 ]]; then
    echo -e "${RED}BLOCKED${NC}: $ERRORS error(s) found"
    echo ""
    echo "Commit aborted. Fix the errors above and try again."
    echo ""
    echo "To skip hooks (NOT RECOMMENDED):"
    echo "  git commit --no-verify"
    echo ""
    exit 1
else
    echo -e "${GREEN}PASSED${NC}: All checks passed"
    echo ""
    exit 0
fi

------------------------------------------------------------
Hook/configuration frameworks

README

### README.md
# ultimate-arr-stack

A Docker Compose media stack for a NAS: request a film or a series, it downloads through a VPN, and it appears in Jellyfin. Ships the *arr apps, two download clients, encrypted local DNS with `.lan` names, HTTPS with auth, monitoring, and tiered backups.

[![License: CC BY-NC 4.0](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-CC%20BY--NC%204.0-lightgrey)](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc/4.0/)

> **This is a fork, and a substantially modified one.** Upstream is [Pharkie/ultimate-arr-stack](https://github.com/Pharkie/ultimate-arr-stack), itself forked from [TheRealCodeVoyage/arr-stack-setup-with-pihole](https://github.com/TheRealCodeVoyage/arr-stack-setup-with-pihole). This tree runs on a Ugreen NAS on a segmented home network, and everything below describes **this** tree — commands are checked against the scripts they ship in, not copied from upstream's README. What changed, and why it matters if you deploy this: [Differences from upstream](#differences-from-upstream).

**Contents:** [What's in the stack](#whats-in-the-stack) · [Architecture](#architecture) · [Requirements](#requirements) · [Quick start](#quick-start) · [Access](#access) · [Common operations](#common-operations) · [Testing](#testing) · [Backups and restore](#backups-and-restore) · [Troubleshooting](#troubleshooting) · [Documentation](#documentation) · [Repository layout](#repository-layout) · [Differences from upstream](#differences-from-upstream) · [Development and verification](#development-and-verification) · [License and attribution](#license-and-attribution)

## What's in the stack

| Group | Services | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Requests | **Seerr** | The front door: users ask for a title, it routes to the right app |
| Library | **Sonarr**, **Radarr** | Series and film management, quality profiles, imports |
| Indexers | **Prowlarr** | Indexer manager; keeps Sonarr/Radarr in sync |
| Subtitles | **Bazarr** | Subtitle search and sync |
| Downloads | **qBittorrent** (torrents), **SABnzbd** (Usenet), **Decypharr** (debrid) | All egress through the VPN except where deliberately not |
| Playback | **Jellyfin** | Media server and apps |
| VPN | **Gluetun**, **vpn-socks5** | WireGuard tunnel for the download clients; SOCKS proxy |
| DNS | **Pi-hole**, **dnscrypt-proxy** | Network-wide DNS + ad blocking, upstream encrypted |
| Edge | **Traefik**, **Tailscale**, **cloudflared** (opt-in) | Reverse proxy, mesh VPN, Cloudflare tunnel |
| Add-ons | **Magnetio** (addon, scraper, redis), **stremio-jellyfin** | Optional extras |
| Operations | **Uptime Kuma**, **Homepage**, **Beszel** + agent, **diun**, **duc**, **configarr**, **deunhealth**, **gluetun-recover**, **gluetun-rotator**, **docker-socket-proxy** | Monitoring, dashboards, disk usage, config-as-code, self-healing |

## Architecture

```
   browser ──▶ Traefik ──────────────┐   HTTPS + basic auth, *.lan names
              (arr-core bridge,      │
               172.20.0.0/24)        │
                                     ▼
   Seerr ──▶ Sonarr / Radarr ──▶ qBittorrent / SABnzbd ──▶ Gluetun ──▶ VPN provider
                    │                                          ▲
                    ▼                                          │
                Jellyfin ◀── media volume (hardlinked, no copies)
                    ▲
   LAN clients ──▶ Pi-hole ──▶ dnscrypt-proxy ──▶ encrypted upstream
```

Three invariants hold the design together:

- **Downloads share Gluetun's network namespace.** A service that should be tunneled is joined to `network_mode: "service:gluetun"`, so a VPN drop stops its traffic rather than leaking it. Recreating Gluetun orphans its dependents — `./scripts/detect-vpn-zombies.sh` exists because those containers keep reporting healthy while having no network at all.
- **One volume, hardlinks.** Media and downloads are siblings on the same filesystem, so an import is a link, not a copy: instant, and no second copy of every file.
- **Pi-hole is a single point of failure by design.** The router hands its address to every DHCP pool, so a Pi-hole that is down is a house with no DNS. That is why restarts never use `docker compose down`, and why the boot reconcile exists.

## Requirements

| | |
|---|---|
| Host | Any Docker host — Ugreen, Synology, QNAP, a Linux box, or a Raspberry Pi 4+. The reference deployment is a Ugreen NAS (aarch64). |
| Docker | Engine + Compose v2, with the ability to run `network_mode: service:*` and macvlan networks |
| Static IP | **Required.** Pi-hole binds `${NAS_IP}:53`; if the address arrives by DHCP after Docker starts, Pi-hole never binds and the network loses DNS |
| Storage | One volume holding `media/` plus `torrents/` and/or `usenet/` as siblings, for hardlinks |
| Secrets | A `.env` (gitignored) built from [.env.example](.env.example) — VPN credentials, app API keys, LAN addresses |
| Optional | Intel/AMD iGPU for hardware transcoding; a Cloudflare account for the tunnel; a Tailscale account; a domain for remote access |

## Quick start

```bash
# 1. Clone
git clone https://github.com/leonardoazeredo/ultimate-arr-stack.git
cd ultimate-arr-stack

# 2. Configure. .env is gitignored and holds every secret and every LAN address.
cp .env.example .env
$EDITOR .env     # NAS_IP, LAN_SUBNET, PUID/PGID, VPN credentials, app API keys

# 3. Bring up the core stack (it creates the arr-core network and DNS first)
docker compose -f docker-compose.arr-stack.yml up -d

# 4. Verify
docker compose -f docker-compose.arr-stack.yml ps      # every service healthy?
dig @<NAS_IP> jellyfin.lan                             # DNS answering?
curl -sI http://<NAS_IP>:8096/System/Info/Public       # Jellyfin responding?
```

Then add the layers you want — each is a separate compose file so one can never take another down:

| Layer | Bring it up | Guide |
|---|---|---|
| Edge: Traefik, `*.lan` DNS, HTTPS with auth | `docker compose -f docker-compose.traefik.yml up -d` | [LOCAL-DNS.md](docs/LOCAL-DNS.md), [HTTPS-LOCAL.md](docs/HTTPS-LOCAL.md) |
| Monitoring, dashboards, configarr, recovery helpers | `docker compose -f docker-compose.utilities.yml up -d` | [UTILITIES.md](docs/UTILITIES.md) |
| Remote access via Tailscale | `docker compose -f docker-compose.tailscale.yml up -d` | [TAILSCALE.md](docs/TAILSCALE.md) |
| Remote access via Cloudflare tunnel | copy `cloudflared/config.yml.example` to `config.yml`, then `docker compose -f docker-compose.cloudflared.yml --profile tunnel up -d` | [REMOTE-ACCESS.md](docs/REMOTE-ACCESS.md) |
| Magnetio add-on | `docker compose -f docker-compose.magnetio.yml up -d` | [UTILITIES.md](docs/UTILITIES.md) |

The cloudflared stack is **opt-in**: it sits behind a `tunnel` profile precisely so a `up -d` over every compose file cannot start a tunnel that has no config to read.

The full walkthrough — directories on the host, app configuration, DNS, HTTPS, remote access — is [docs/SETUP.md](docs/SETUP.md). App setup is either [script-assisted](docs/APP-CONFIG-QUICK.md) or [manual](docs/APP-CONFIG.md).

## Access

| Service | LAN | `.lan` (with the edge layer) | Remote |
|---|---|---|---|
| Jellyfin | `NAS_IP:8096` | `https://jellyfin.lan` | yes, if exposed |
| Seerr | `NAS_IP:5055` | `https://seerr.lan` | yes, if exposed |
| Sonarr / Radarr / Prowlarr / Bazarr | `NAS_IP:8989` / `:7878` / `:9696` / `:6767` | `https://sonarr.lan`, `https://radarr.lan`, `https://prowlarr.lan`, `https://bazarr.lan` | LAN only |
| qBittorrent / SABnzbd | `NAS_IP:8085` / `:8082` | `https://qbit.lan`, `https://sabnzbd.lan` | LAN only |
| Pi-hole admin | `NAS_IP:8081/admin` | `https://pihole.lan` | LAN only |
| Uptime Kuma | `NAS_IP:3001` | `https://uptime.lan` | LAN only |
| Traefik dashboard | — (reached through Traefik) | `https://traefik.lan` | LAN only |
| Homepage / Beszel / duc | — (no published port; reached through Traefik) | `https://homepage.lan`, `https://beszel.lan`, `https://duc.lan` | LAN only |

The `.lan` names need the edge layer, and the `https` URLs need its auth middleware; Jellyfin and Seerr keep their own app-level login on top. The complete matrix, including which services are deliberately unpublished, is [docs/REFERENCE.md](docs/REFERENCE.md).

## Common operations

| Task | Command |
|---|---|
| Health at a glance | `docker compose -f docker-compose.arr-stack.yml ps` |
| Restart safely — **never** `down` | `./scripts/restart-stack.sh [all\|arr\|traefik\|utilities\|magnetio\|cloudflared]` |
| Reconcile every stack after a reboot | `./scripts/boot-compose-up.sh` |
| Prove traffic is going through the VPN | `./scripts/check-vpn.sh` |
| Find containers stranded on a dead VPN namespace | `./scripts/detect-vpn-zombies.sh` |
| Detect the credential-propagation drift class | `./scripts/detect-credential-drift.sh` |
| Configure the apps through their APIs | `./scripts/configure-apps.sh` |
| Clear stuck download-queue items | `./scripts/queue-cleanup.sh` |
| Repair Sonarr folder names / Radarr paths | `./scripts/fix-sonarr-folders.sh`, `./scripts/fix-radarr-paths.sh` |
| Back up config volumes | `./scripts/arr-backup.sh` |
| Apply tiered retention to backups | `./scripts/backup-prune.sh` |
| Deploy the current branch to the host | `./scripts/sync-nas.sh` |
| Update images | [docs/UPGRADING.md](docs/UPGRADING.md) |

> ⚠️ **Two commands this repo never runs, and you should not either.** `docker compose down` takes Pi-hole with it and therefore kills DNS for the whole network; and `--remove-orphans` deletes every container from the *other* compose files, because this stack's services share a project name across files. Both have caused real outages — see [docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md](docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md).

## Testing

Three layers, and none of them needs a cloud CI account:

| Layer | Run it with | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| Static + behavioural (bats) | `./tests/run-tests.sh` | Compose validity, duplicate ports and IPs, pinned images, secret hygiene, `.env` documentation, script behaviour, hook wiring. Needs no Docker and no host access. It ends with a census — `executed / skipped / failed`, the reasons for the skips, and a warning when the git-gated tests produced no verdict on this host. Read that line; "ok" is not the same as "covered". |
| Python toolkit | `./tests/toolkit/pytest.sh` | The `scripts/lib/*.py` modules. Exits **77**, never 0, when Docker is unavailable — an absent oracle must not read as a passing one. |
| End-to-end (Playwright) | `npm run test:e2e` | Real HTTP, UI, VPN-egress and DNS behaviour against a live stack. Sixteen of its tests need the Docker socket, so the full run happens on the host itself. A floor reporter fails any full-suite run that executed fewer than 30 tests: a missing or rotted `.env.e2e` used to produce a green run that had executed almost nothing. |

**CI runs the deterministic layers for you** on every push and pull request: the bats suite above (its census is written into the job summary), the mutation corpus entries that guard the files a change touches, `actionlint` over the workflow files, and `terraform fmt -check`. The expensive halves run nightly or on dispatch — the full corpus, the universalmutator discovery sweep, and the kcov coverage diagnostic. Nothing that needs the live host is in that workflow: the end-to-end suite, the deploy pipeline and the router-segmentation assertion stay out by design.

Guards here are **proved able to fail**, not assumed to work. [tests/mutation/](tests/mutation/) holds a corpus of defects this repo actually shipped, which the suite must kill; a generated sweep hunts for code no test guards; and `tests/mutation-corpus.bats` re-checks in seconds that every corpus pattern still changes its target. After touching a guard, run `./tests/mutation/run-mutations.sh` — and the manual deploy workflow does exactly that for the guards a change touches. The rules that keep this honest are written down in [tests/mutation/README.md](tests/mutation/README.md) and [docs/TEST-HARDENING-LOG.md](docs/TEST-HARDENING-LOG.md).

## Backups and restore

`./scripts/arr-backup.sh` archives the config volumes that cannot be regenerated — app databases, credentials, hand-edited resolver and DNS config — to a single tarball; `./scripts/backup-prune.sh` then applies grandfather-father-son tiers so the directory does not grow without bound. Coverage and caveats: [docs/BACKUP.md](docs/BACKUP.md). Rebuilding a host from a backup: [docs/RESTORE.md](docs/RESTORE.md).

## Troubleshooting

[docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md](docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md) is the full list. The four that cost the most time here:

- **DNS is dead but Pi-hole looks fine.** The container is running, healthy, and serving nobody, because its published ports vanished. The tell is an empty `PORTS` column in `docker ps` and nothing listening on `NAS_IP:53`.
- **Downloads stopped, everything is green.** A VPN recreate orphaned its dependents; containers that only check localhost keep reporting healthy with no network. Assert on egress, not on health status.
- **A reboot left services unreachable.** Docker restores `restart: always` containers itself, and address-pinned bindings can silently fail. `./scripts/boot-compose-up.sh` reconciles every stack; it is worth having scheduled at boot.
- **The stack came up half-missing.** Almost always `--remove-orphans`, or a service recreated through the wrong compose file.

| [BACKUP.md](docs/BACKUP.md) / [RESTORE.md](docs/RESTORE.md) | Protecting and rebuilding config |
| [TROUBLESHOOTING.md](docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md) | Symptom-first fixes |
| [HOME-ASSISTANT.md](docs/HOME-ASSISTANT.md) | Completion notifications |
| [MIGRATION-arr-off-vpn.md](docs/MIGRATION-arr-off-vpn.md) | Why Sonarr and Radarr are deliberately outside the tunnel |
| [EXIT-NODE-PROJECT-LOG.md](docs/EXIT-NODE-PROJECT-LOG.md) / [TEST-HARDENING-LOG.md](docs/TEST-HARDENING-LOG.md) | Audited project logs: what shipped, what was wrong, what is still open |
| [LEGAL.md](docs/LEGAL.md) | Intended use and disclaimer |

## Repository layout

```
docker-compose.arr-stack.yml     core: VPN, apps, downloads, DNS, media
docker-compose.traefik.yml       reverse proxy + macvlan for *.lan
docker-compose.utilities.yml     monitoring, dashboards, configarr, recover/rotate helpers
docker-compose.tailscale.yml     Tailscale subnet router
docker-compose.cloudflared.yml   optional Cloudflare tunnel (opt-in profile)
docker-compose.magnetio.yml      Magnetio add-on, scraper and redis
scripts/                         operations, one concern per script
tests/                           bats suite, Python toolkit, Playwright e2e, mutation framework
docs/                            every guide, plus the audited project logs
pihole/dnsmasq.d/                local DNS records
traefik/dynamic/                 routers and middleware
terraform/                       app configuration as code
```

## Differences from upstream

Deploying this fork is not the same as deploying upstream's. The load-bearing differences:

- **The Docker network is `arr-core`** (upstream: `arr-stack`), and compose project/volume names are pinned explicitly so a rename cannot orphan data. Anything referencing the old network name needs updating.
- **DNS is encrypted end to end.** Pi-hole forwards to the in-stack `dnscrypt-proxy` rather than to a public resolver in the clear.
- **The Cloudflare tunnel is opt-in** behind a compose profile, so an unconditional `up -d` cannot start a tunnel with no configuration and crash-loop it.
- **The Tailscale exit-node role runs on the router**, not in this stack; the NAS-side implementation was built, measured, and decommissioned. The history and the numbers are in [docs/EXIT-NODE-PROJECT-LOG.md](docs/EXIT-NODE-PROJECT-LOG.md).
- **A much heavier verification harness**: a mutation corpus with a triage ledger, a containerised Python and coverage toolkit, an executed-count floor on the end-to-end suite, and a skip census in the bats runner.
- **Additional operations scripts**: boot reconciliation, VPN zombie detection, credential-drift detection, branch deployment, Sonarr/Radarr repair.

## Development and verification

**LLM-generated, human-reviewed.** This code was written with [Claude Code](https://claude.ai/claude-code) (Anthropic), planned, directed and verified by the human author, whose review extends past the generated output to the tests and the deployments. It has still had limited manual review — make your own checks before trusting it with your library.

Work lands the same way every time: a **feature branch**, tested against the real deployment before it can reach `main`, and `main` deployed from git rather than by copying files. The full rule, including why testing happens on the host rather than in CI, is in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md).

## License and attribution

Documentation, configuration files and examples are licensed [CC BY-NC 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc/4.0/) (Attribution-NonCommercial). The bundled applications (Sonarr, Radarr, Jellyfin, and the rest) keep their own licenses.

Forked from [Pharkie/ultimate-arr-stack](https://github.com/Pharkie/ultimate-arr-stack), itself forked from [TheRealCodeVoyage/arr-stack-setup-with-pihole](https://github.com/TheRealCodeVoyage/arr-stack-setup-with-pihole).

This project provides configuration for **legal, open-source software** for managing a personal media library. See [docs/LEGAL.md](docs/LEGAL.md) for intended use, your responsibilities, and the disclaimer.

### .env.example
# =============================================================================
# ENVIRONMENT CONFIGURATION
# =============================================================================
# Organized by setup level. Fill in sections for your chosen setup:
#   - Core: VPN-protected downloads + Jellyfin (required)
#   - + local DNS: Adds .lan domains and ad-blocking (optional)
#   - + remote access: Adds external access from anywhere (optional)
# =============================================================================

# =============================================================================
# CORE (required for all setups)
# =============================================================================

# Media storage root path - adjust for YOUR NAS/server
# Examples:
#   /volume1/data      (Ugreen, Synology)
#   /share/data        (QNAP)
#   /srv/data          (Linux server)
# Stack directory - where this repo lives on the NAS
# Used by scripts for paths to compose files, configs, and logs
# Examples:
#   /volume1/docker/arr-stack   (Ugreen/Synology volume 1)
#   /volume2/docker/arr-stack   (Ugreen/Synology volume 2)
#   /share/docker/arr-stack     (QNAP)
#   /srv/docker/arr-stack       (Linux server)
NAS_STACK_DIR=/volume1/docker/arr-stack

# Media storage root path - adjust for YOUR NAS/server
# Examples:
#   /volume1/data      (Ugreen, Synology)
#   /share/data        (QNAP)
#   /srv/data          (Linux server)
MEDIA_ROOT=/volume1/data

# Timezone - used for correct scheduling, logs, and UI times across all services
# Find yours at: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tz_database_time_zones
# Examples: America/New_York, America/Los_Angeles, Europe/Paris, Australia/Sydney
TZ=Europe/London

# User/Group IDs (usually 1000/1000, check with: id -u && id -g)
PUID=1000
PGID=1000

# Hardware Transcoding: not used on this NAS (DH4300 Plus is Rockchip RK3588,
# not Intel). RKMPP hw transcoding is configured directly via commented-out
# device passthrough in docker-compose.arr-stack.yml's jellyfin service - see
# the comments there before enabling. No env var needed for it.

# VPN Configuration - Proton VPN via WireGuard
# https://github.com/qdm12/gluetun-wiki/blob/main/setup/providers/protonvpn.md
VPN_SERVICE_PROVIDER=protonvpn

# VPN Type (wireguard or openvpn)
VPN_TYPE=wireguard

# Get both values from: https://account.protonvpn.com/downloads → WireGuard configuration
# Generate a config (router/Linux, NAT-PMP/port forwarding ON if you want VPN_PORT_FORWARDING),
# download the .conf, and copy PrivateKey and Address from its [Interface] section.
WIREGUARD_PRIVATE_KEY=your_wireguard_private_key_here
WIREGUARD_ADDRESSES=your_wireguard_address_here

# Example for OpenVPN-based providers (NordVPN, PIA, etc.):
# OPENVPN_USER=your_username
# OPENVPN_PASSWORD=your_password

# VPN Server Location
# Pick based on your needs:
#   - Closest to you = fastest speeds
#   - Specific country = access region-locked content (e.g., US for US Netflix)
# Examples: United Kingdom, United States, Netherlands, Switzerland
# NOTE: Some countries legally block public indexers at the Cloudflare/ISP level
#   (e.g. the UK returns HTTP 451 for EZTV and similar). If new releases never
#   grab and Prowlarr disables an indexer for "failures", switch to a non-blocking
#   country here. See docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md "New Releases Never Grab".
VPN_COUNTRIES=Netherlands

# How often gluetun-rotator restarts gluetun so it re-picks a WireGuard exit
# server from VPN_COUNTRIES above, instead of staying pinned to whichever
# server it first connected to. Default 21600 = 6 hours.
GLUETUN_ROTATE_INTERVAL_SECONDS=21600

# =============================================================================
# + LOCAL DNS (optional - adds .lan domains and ad-blocking)
# =============================================================================
# Enables access like http://sonarr.lan instead of http://192.168.1.50:8989
# Skip this section for Core setup (IP:port access only)

# Your NAS IP address (used for Pi-hole DNS binding)
# IMPORTANT: This must be a static IP set in your NAS network settings, not just a DHCP
# reservation on your router. A DHCP reservation gives you the same IP every time, but the
# NAS still asks for it at boot — Docker starts before that handshake finishes, and Pi-hole
# fails to bind (exit 128) on every reboot.
#
# To check:  ip addr show eth0  — if you see "dynamic", it's DHCP.
# To fix:    Edit /etc/network/interfaces.d/ifcfg-eth0 and change "iface eth0 inet dhcp"
#            to "iface eth0 inet static" with address/netmask/gateway lines, then reboot.
#            See docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md for full steps.
# Also keep: a DHCP reservation on your router for this IP, so no other device gets it.
#
# Find your current IP with: hostname -I | awk '{print $1}'
NAS_IP=192.168.1.102

# Pi-hole Admin Password
PIHOLE_UI_PASS=your_secure_pihole_password

# Traefik's LAN IP and MAC (via macvlan network)
# This gives Traefik its own IP on your network where it can use port 80
# Choose an unused IP in your LAN range, then add a DHCP reservation in your router
TRAEFIK_LAN_IP=192.168.1.11

# Fixed MAC address for DHCP reservation (can be any valid locally-administered MAC)
# Format: x2:xx:xx:xx:xx:xx where first octet's second nibble is 2,6,A,E (local bit set)
TRAEFIK_LAN_MAC=02:42:c0:a8:01:0b

# Network interface name on the NAS (check with: ip link show)
# Common values: eth0, bond0, enp0s3
LAN_INTERFACE=eth0

# Your LAN subnet and gateway
# LAN_SUBNET must be EXACTLY ONE CIDR. It is the subnet of Traefik's macvlan
# network, which cannot parse a list -- appending a second subnet here is legal
# for the gluetun killswitch but destroys the macvlan on its next recreate,
# taking every .lan hostname down with it. Use EXTRA_LAN_SUBNETS instead.
# Example: If your router is 192.168.1.1 and devices are 192.168.1.x:
#   LAN_SUBNET=192.168.1.0/24
#   LAN_GATEWAY=192.168.1.1
LAN_SUBNET=192.168.1.0/24
LAN_GATEWAY=192.168.1.1

# Further LAN subnets, if your network is VLAN-segmented (comma-separated, no
# spaces). Read ONLY by gluetun's outbound firewall, which accepts a list.
# Never by the Traefik macvlan above. Advertising a subnet on the tailnet is a
# separate decision -- that is TS_EXTRA_ROUTES, below.
# Example: EXTRA_LAN_SUBNETS=192.168.120.0/24
# EXTRA_LAN_SUBNETS=

# =============================================================================
# + REMOTE ACCESS (optional - adds external access from anywhere)
# =============================================================================
# Enables access like https://jellyfin.yourdomain.com from outside your home
# Skip this section if you only need local access

# Domain Configuration
# Buy your own domain (~$10/year). Cloudflare Registrar is simplest: https://www.cloudflare.com/products/registrar/
DOMAIN=yourdomain.com

# Traefik Dashboard Authentication
# Generate with: htpasswd -nbB admin 'YOUR_PASSWORD'
# IMPORTANT: Must be quoted (contains $ which Docker interprets as variables)
TRAEFIK_DASHBOARD_AUTH='admin:$2y$05$your_bcrypt_hash_here'

# Tailscale (optional - reach the whole LAN remotely, including CGNAT/hotel WiFi)
# See docs/TAILSCALE.md for the full walkthrough. Only needed if you deploy
# docker-compose.tailscale.yml.

# Tailscale node hostname as it appears in the admin console
# TS_HOSTNAME=arr-stack-nas

# Pre-auth key for unattended setup (https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/keys)
# Leave unset for interactive first-run auth — recommended for personal use
# TS_AUTHKEY=tskey-auth-xxxxxxxxxxxx

# Additional subnets to advertise beyond LAN_SUBNET (comma-separated, no spaces)
# Example: also advertise the Docker arr-stack subnet for raw container access
# TS_EXTRA_ROUTES=172.20.0.0/24

# ProtonVPN exit node: no longer a NAS/.env concern. The Tailscale exit-node
# role now runs natively on arr-stack-router (its own WireGuard + Tailscale
# config, configured directly on the device) — see
# docs/EXIT-NODE-PROJECT-LOG.md. The NAS-based gluetun-exit/tailscale-exit
# pair this section used to configure was decommissioned once the router
# path proved an 8-10x throughput improvement.

# =============================================================================
# UTILITIES (optional)
# =============================================================================

# Plex (if you swap Jellyfin for Plex — see docs/SETUP.md "Prefer Plex?" section)
# Get claim token from: https://plex.tv/claim (expires in 4 minutes!)
# Only needed on first run to link server to your Plex account
# PLEX_CLAIM=claim-xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx


# Beszel agent key (for beszel-agent in utilities.yml)
# Get from Beszel hub UI: Add System → copy KEY value
# BESZEL_KEY=

# DIUN - Docker Image Update Notifier (for diun in utilities.yml)
# Checks registries for new image versions and sends push notifications via ntfy.sh
# DIUN_SCHEDULE=0 6 * * *                              # When to check (cron, default: daily 6am)
# DIUN_NTFY_TOPIC=arr-stack-diun-changeme               # Diun won't start without this. Pick a random/unguessable
                                                         # topic name (ntfy.sh topics are public unless self-hosted
                                                         # with auth) and subscribe to it at https://ntfy.sh/<topic>
                                                         # in the app or website.

# Sonarr/Radarr/Prowlarr API keys - generate with `openssl rand -hex 16`.
# These are the *declarative* keys: set via SONARR__AUTH__APIKEY/
# RADARR__AUTH__APIKEY/PROWLARR__AUTH__APIKEY env vars in
# docker-compose.arr-stack.yml, so the app adopts this key on every
# container start instead of generating its own. Also consumed by configarr
# in utilities.yml and by terraform/ for cross-app credential propagation.
SONARR_API_KEY=generate_with_openssl_rand_hex_16
RADARR_API_KEY=generate_with_openssl_rand_hex_16
PROWLARR_API_KEY=generate_with_openssl_rand_hex_16

# Homepage dashboard - widget credentials (for homepage in utilities.yml).
# Sonarr/Radarr/Prowlarr above are reused directly. Leave these blank here;
# populate real values on the NAS from Bitwarden or each app's own settings
# UI - never write live secret values into this file yourself.
# BAZARR_API_KEY=            # Bazarr Settings > General > API Key
# SABNZBD_API_KEY=           # SABnzbd Config > General > API Key
# QBITTORRENT_USER=          # qBittorrent WebUI username (default: admin)
# QBITTORRENT_PASS=          # qBittorrent WebUI password
# JELLYFIN_API_KEY=          # Jellyfin Dashboard > API Keys (current rotated key)
# SEERR_API_KEY=             # Seerr Settings > General > API Key
# PIHOLE_API_TOKEN=          # Pi-hole Settings > API/Web interface > app password (v6)
# UPTIME_KUMA_SLUG=          # Uptime Kuma > Status Pages > (create one) > slug in its URL

# TorBox - debrid download client (for decypharr in docker-compose.arr-stack.yml)
# Get API key from: https://torbox.app/settings
# TORBOX_API_KEY=

# Stremio-Jellyfin - Stremio addon exposing this Jellyfin library as a stream
# source (for stremio-jellyfin in docker-compose.arr-stack.yml)
# Use a dedicated Jellyfin user if you don't want to reuse your admin password.
# STREMIO_JELLYFIN_USER=
# STREMIO_JELLYFIN_PASSWORD=

# Magnetio - Stremio addon for torrent/debrid fallback streams (for the addon
# and scraper services in docker-compose.magnetio.yml). Redis is internal-only
# but still needs a password since it's reachable on the arr-stack network.
# MAGNETIO_REDIS_PASSWORD=
#
# REQUIRED, not optional: magnetio-addon protects /stats and its swagger-stats
# middleware with basic auth that falls back to the published upstream default
# admin/magnetio when these are unset. docker-compose.magnetio.yml uses the
# `${VAR:?}` required form, so compose refuses to start without them rather
# than serving metrics on default credentials.
# METRICS_USER=
# METRICS_PASSWORD=

# VPN-SOCKS5 - SOCKS5 proxy tunneled through Gluetun's VPN connection (for
# vpn-socks5 in docker-compose.arr-stack.yml). Internal-only (reachable at
# gluetun:1080 on the arr-stack network) but still needs auth since any
# container on that network could otherwise use it.
# VPN_SOCKS5_USER=
# VPN_SOCKS5_PASSWORD=
